# Golden set — adjudication sheet

Generated 2026-10-01 by `scripts/adjudication_sheet.py`. Calls are made on the adjudication page (`docs/golden-adjudication.html`, published as an artifact) and exported to `data/golden/work/adjudications.json`, which `scripts/build_golden.py` reads. This file is the reference copy.

**Real rows:** R = responsive to the request as written, N = not responsive. Responsiveness only; privilege and exemptions do not matter here.
**Synthetic rows:** K = keep the intended label, F = flip it, D = drop the email from the set.

## Request 24-409 — Juli Beth Hinds / UCSD USP — County LUEG records

Filed April 16, 2024 · Clerk of the Board of Supervisors (COB), Parks & Recreation (DPR), Planning & Development Services (PDS), Department of Public Works (DPW) · https://pra.sandiegocounty.gov/requests/24-409

**Window:** Jan 1, 2023 – Jul 31, 2023 (refined scope of 5/17/24)

- **Item 2.** Anything that includes Juli Beth Hinds, Dr. Amy Lerner, Urban Studies and Planning (USP), Global Policy & Strategy (GPS), or UCSD in conjunction with any of those terms.
- **Item 3.** Anything related to the Regional Water Equity Report (RWER) or the RDF (the County's Regional Decarbonization Framework).
- **Item 4.** Anything referencing Hinds, Lerner, Geosyntec, USP, GPS, the RWER or the RDF (UCSD in conjunction with any of those) sent to or from: Elise Ruiz; anyone at Murakawa Communications; anyone at Geosyntec (incl. Ken Susillo, Megan Otto, Stephanie Zinn); anyone at the County (incl. Stephanie Gaines, Crystal Benham, Todd Snyder, Sarah Agassi, Eden Brukman, Murtaza Baxamusa).

Item (1) of the refinement is the date window. The original request (Hinds; UCSD USP; Nathan Fletcher; 1/1/2022 to 4/16/2024) was superseded by the 5/17/24 refinement.

<details><summary>Full request text</summary>

```text
Updated request scope as of 5/17/24 refinement sent by Mr.Small:

We have put some thought into search restrictions and terms and here is what we would suggest:

(1) total time range of January 1, 2023 through July 31, 2023
(2) Anything that includes Juli Beth Hinds, Dr. Amy Lerner, urban studies and planning (USP), global policy & strategy (GPS), UCSD in conjunction with any of those terms
(3) Anything related to the regional water equity report or RDF
(4) Anything containing a reference to "Juli Beth Hinds, Dr Amy Lerner,  Geosyntec, USP, GPS, or the RWER or RDF", UCSD in conjunction with any of those terms sent to or from "Elise Ruiz, anyone at Murakawa Communications, anyone at Geosyntec including but not limited to Ken Susillo, Megan Otto, Stephanie Zinn; and anyone at the County including but not limited to Stephanie Gaines, Crystal Benham, Todd Snyder, Sarah Agassi, Eden Brukman, and Murtaza Baxamusa"

Original Request:

To Whom it may Concern:
I am writing on behalf of my client Juli Beth Hinds. On behalf of Ms. Hinds, please consider this a formal request pursuant to California Public Records Act, Government Code §6250 et seq. for copies of all DOCUMENTS of the County of San Diego Land Use & Environment Group that reference or relate to: Juli Beth Hinds; the Department of Urban Studies and Planning at the University of California San Diego; or Nathan Fletcher. ("DOCUMENTS" as used herein, shall be defined to mean any ''writing'' as that term is defined in Evidence Code section 250, including, but not limited to, correspondence, emails, texts, communications of any kind, recordings, tapes, computer print-outs, records, slides, photographs, notes, working papers, written analyses, memoranda, plans, telegraphs, telexes and transcriptions, electronic data, and notes or memoranda made by any telephone communications.) This request encompasses all DOCUMENTS dated or created between the dates of January 1, 2022 through the date of this letter.
I request delivery of these records to our firm's office, located at 402 W. Broadway, Suite 1270, San Diego, CA 92101, or we can arrange to pick them up at a reasonable location designated by the County.

Thank you for your attention to this matter.
```

</details>

## Request 25-3152 — Alpine Community Planning Group communications

Filed June  6, 2025 · Planning & Development Services (PDS) · https://pra.sandiegocounty.gov/requests/25-3152

**Window:** Jan 1, 2024 – May 31, 2025 (every item)

- **Item 1.** All ACPG agendas and minutes.
- **Item 2.** All communications between any ACPG member and another ACPG member.
- **Item 3.** All communications between any ACPG member and another government agency, including the County.
- **Item 4.** All communications between any ACPG member and any member of the public, including individuals or entities with business before the group.

Current and former members during the window count (see roster). The request is addressed to the ACPG members; there is no subject-matter limit on items 2–4.

<details><summary>Full request text</summary>

```text
Re: Public Records Act Request

Dear Alpine Community Planning Group Members,

Pursuant to the California Public Records Act, I am writing to request a copy of the records detailed below.

I am researching how the Alpine Community Planning Group (ACPG) conducts its business on the part of the residents of Alpine in preparation for publication of an expose. To that end, I request the following records:

1. All Alpine Community Planning Group Agendas and Minutes from 1 January 2024 through 31 May 2025.

2. All communications between any member of the Alpine Community Planning Group and another member(s) of the Alpine Community Planning Group from 1 January 2024 through 31 May 2025. Please include current and former members during this time period.

3. All communications between any member of the Alpine Community Planning Group and another government agency, including between the group members and the County of San Diego from 1 January 2024 through 31 May 2025. Please include current and former members during this time period.

4. All communications between any member of the Alpine Community Planning Group and any member of the public, including between the group members and individuals/entities with business before the group from 1 January 2024 through 31 May 2025. Please include current and former members during this time period.

Please note that the Public Records Act allows a member of the public to request records by describing their content, rather than asking for specific documents by name; an agency that receives such a request must “search for records based on criteria set forth in the search request.”

Public records represent “any handwriting, typewriting, printing, photostating, photographing, photocopying, transmitting by electronic mail or facsimile, and every other means of recording upon any tangible thing any form of communication or representation, including letters, words, pictures, sounds, or symbols, or combinations thereof, and any record thereby created, regardless of the manner in which the record has been stored.” (Government Code section 7920.545.)

1. This request applies to all records in your agency’s possession, including documents created by a member of another government agency or a member of the public.

2. If specific portions of any documents are exempt from disclosure, please provide the non-exempt portions.

3. Please provide entire documents, even if only parts of them are responsive to this request.

If you maintain records or data in electronic format, please provide them in that same format.

1 California First Amendment Coalition v. Superior Court, 67 Cal.App.4th 159, 165-66 (1998).
2 See California State University v. Superior Court, 90 Cal. App. 4th 810, 824-25 (1999).
3 Gov’t. Code § 6253(a). versions are acceptable) to avoid copying costs.
4 If you are unable to reproduce electronic records in electronic form, please provide a reason for doing so.

I request that you waive copying fees because I have limited resources, and the information requested will be used in the public interest to further the public’s understanding of the local community planning group action. None of the information obtained will be sold or distributed for profit. If you are unable to waive the copying fees, and you anticipate that these costs will exceed $50, or that the time needed to copy the records will delay their release, please contact me so that we can arrange to inspect the document or decide which documents we wish to have copied. Otherwise, please copy and send them as soon as possible. Please respond to this request within ten (10) days, either by providing all the requested records or by providing a written response setting forth the legal authority for withholding or redacting any document and stating when the documents will be made available.

If you require any clarification in identifying responsive documents or focusing this request, please contact [redacted]. Please send all records responsive to this request to Louis Russo.

Thank you for your time and attention to this matter.

Sincerely,

[redacted]

Gov’t. Code § 6253.9.
Gov’t. Code §§ 6253(c), 6255.
Gov’t. Code § 6253.1.
```

</details>

On Tue, Aug 2, 2022 at 6:58 PM Baxamusa, Murtaza &lt;Murtaza.Baxamusa@sdcounty.ca.gov<mailto:Murtaza.Baxamusa@sdcounty.ca.gov>&gt; wrote:
Dear UCSD team,

There is a departmental sustainability planning effort underway at the County that is quite unique, in that the plans were built up from the bottom-up by the staff working on the activities within the departments themselves rather than a high-level plan that may have difficulty getting actually implemented. Another unique feature of this effort is that there is a countywide steering committee with County staff that spans all the four functional groups covering all departments, so that there is coordination at the highest levels.

This departmental sustainability effort was directed by the Board last May, and the countywide sustainability goals were adopted in March this year; so the work (with Arup as consultant) has been proceeding somewhat on parallel track with the RDF.

Nicole from our office is leading this effort, and I wanted you all to be acquainted with it (see her email below) since it may come up in our meetings on RDF implementation and USP workshops. If you would like we can set up a meeting with Nicole.

Please let me know if you need any other background on this,
Murtaza

On Tue, Aug 2, 2022 at 6:58 PM Baxamusa, Murtaza &lt;Murtaza.Baxamusa@sdcounty.ca.gov<mailto:Murtaza.Baxamusa@sdcounty.ca.gov>&gt; wrote:
Dear UCSD team,

There is a departmental sustainability planning effort underway at the County that is quite unique, in that the plans were built up from the bottom-up by the staff working on the activities within the departments themselves rather than a high-level plan that may have difficulty getting actually implemented. Another unique feature of this effort is that there is a countywide steering committee with County staff that spans all the four functional groups covering all departments, so that there is coordination at the highest levels.

This departmental sustainability effort was directed by the Board last May, and the countywide sustainability goals were adopted in March this year; so the work (with Arup as consultant) has been proceeding somewhat on parallel track with the RDF.

Nicole from our office is leading this effort, and I wanted you all to be acquainted with it (see her email below) since it may come up in our meetings on RDF implementation and USP workshops. If you would like we can set up a meeting with Nicole.

Please let me know if you need any other background on this,
Murtaza

  ________________________________

- **Original:** https://pra.sandiegocounty.gov/documents/33733431/download

```text
Dear Murtaza, Rebeca and Nicole,

Thanks for the very useful conversation today.  As follow-up, I’ve drafted the attached concept note on what benchmarking the County’s efforts against the SDGs could look like.  I stress that this is meant to be complementary to the existing sustainability framework used by the county, but the benefit is that it benchmarks the county’s progress against internationally-recognized goals for environmental sustainability and social inclusion.

I’d be eager to hear your feedback and incorporate your advice.  I’m excited about a data-driven, integrated strategic planning process to meet the SDGs, using the common framework they provide and the quantitative 2030 as an useful organizing principle for some of our conversations (again, as a complement to existing county vision and goals around sustainability).  The SDGs also open the door to strategic collaborations with other jurisdictions in the region to meet the SDGs (including the City of San Diego, which has announced it wants to do a VLR but has not yet started).

Regards,

Gordon

PS Copying Jeff Myers who supports me on SDGPI work.

From: Baxamusa, Murtaza &lt;Murtaza.Baxamusa@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;

Dear UCSD team,

I wanted to send a reminder and agenda for tomorrow’s call. The purpose is to understand the intersections between the various sustainability efforts in the County and region; and see how all of our efforts can align with the work already being done.

We won’t be having a presentation of all these efforts; instead use our limited time to discuss and answer questions. If you would like to view the Board presentation from March 1, please click on this link: Board of Supervisors Meeting (granicus.com)<https://urldefense.com/v3/__https:/sdcounty.granicus.com/player/clip/3182?view_id=9&redirect=true__;!!Mih3wA!DFGRE4yvyoIGJn9nYHce6B3f8y3b7XA6BtOzfzsyNspQb8e2mnJX1pHI9okayPhOiKGP_oHFIJ-FTSHT7Gzl25eGBSF14mPF4zE$> (starts at 2:35:00)

   3)      Sustainability roundtables

Good afternoon everyone,

We hope you are as excited as we are for the workshop on Monday. Below are some logistical pieces of information for Monday.

Date: Monday, November 7
Time: 8:15-9 Arrival, registration. Begins at 9:00 (Agenda with detailed times on the attached)
Attire: Casual
Refreshments: Refreshments and lunch is provided
Parking: Taking the trolley, rideshare, or carpooling is strongly encouraged.  UCSD has been adjusting parking rates, availability, and incentives for transit to discourage driving - which is part of their overall climate plan!
      Directions to 6th College &#8211; From North Torrey Pines Road turn right on Muir College Drive. You will see some excited UCSD students that will show you which lot to park in holding signs for the SD County RDF Workshop. They will guide you to 1 of 4 options where you will park in an &quot;A&quot; space.
      Option 1 &#8211; parking lot for 6th College, with elevator right up to 7th floor.
      Options 2 &amp; 3 &#8211; Lots P302 &amp; 303, on the corners of Muir College Drive and Scholars Drive North.
      Option 4 &#8211; Pangea Parking Lot on Pangea Drive and Scholars Drive North (see attached document).

      You will need to have the Parkmobile app with your car registered to enter a code for parking. Make a Parkmobile account HERE.<https://urldefense.com/v3/__https:/app.parkmobile.io/register?redirect=*2Fzone*2Fstart__;JSU!!Mih3wA!A9eFHBIGDl3DwEC04Z-QjaS1GcBaVBSvfKyUQU0PW5omtE9Q2Y3sj137OpTo2NyJtzX4EftfJ9OlyTkutg$>
      App:   Parkmobile from apple<https://urldefense.com/v3/__https:/apps.apple.com/us/app/parkmobile-paid-parking-made/id365399299__;!!Mih3wA!A9eFHBIGDl3DwEC04Z-QjaS1GcBaVBSvfKyUQU0PW5omtE9Q2Y3sj137OpTo2NyJtzX4EftfJ9NnZ29D4A$>  Parkmobile from Googl<https://urldefense.com/v3/__https:/play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=net.sharewire.parkmobilev2__;!!Mih3wA!A9eFHBIGDl3DwEC04Z-QjaS1GcBaVBSvfKyUQU0PW5omtE9Q2Y3sj137OpTo2NyJtzX4EftfJ9P-gp9i1A$>e Play  Parkmobile from Microsoft<https://urldefense.com/v3/__https:/www.microsoft.com/en-us/p/parkmobile/9nblggh08v9c?rtc=1&activetab=pivot:overviewtab__;!!Mih3wA!A9eFHBIGDl3DwEC04Z-QjaS1GcBaVBSvfKyUQU0PW5omtE9Q2Y3sj137OpTo2NyJtzX4EftfJ9ML5Ydawg$>. UCSD will provide the code once you arrive.

Please reach out if you have any questions, see you Monday.

From: Baxamusa, Murtaza &lt;Murtaza.Baxamusa@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;
Sent: Wednesday, November 2, 2022 7:08 AM
To: Cooper, Garrett &lt;Garrett.Cooper@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Dang, Ha &lt;Ha.Dang@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Alfaro, Suedy &lt;Suedy.Alfaro@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Lintvedt, Inga &lt;Inga.Lintvedt@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Sjoblom, Randall &lt;Randall.Sjoblom@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Beaumont, Anne &lt;Anne.Beaumont@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Abbott, Jamie &lt;Jamie.Abbott@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Buonomo, Heather &lt;Heather.Buonomo@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Glueck, John-Ross &lt;John-Ross.Glueck@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Harbert, Amy &lt;Amy.Harbert@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Appel, Rebeca &lt;Rebeca.Appel@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; De La Rosa, Michael &lt;Michael.DeLaRosa@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Demorest, Erin &lt;Erin.Demorest@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Hanson, Elise A &lt;EliseA.Hanson@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Acosta, Migell &lt;Migell.Acosta@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Albright, Brian &lt;Brian.Albright@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Oakes, Chelsea &lt;Chelsea.Oakes@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Salomon, Johanna &lt;Johanna.Salomon@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Trojanowska, Kasia &lt;Kasia.Trojanowska@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Bray, Kelly &lt;Kelly.Bray@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Farmer, Tyler &lt;Tyler.Farmer@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Slovick, Mark &lt;Mark.Slovick@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Talleh, Rami &lt;Rami.Talleh@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Benham, Crystal &lt;Crystal.Benham@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; James, Kamilah &lt;kamilah.James@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Gade, Derek &lt;Derek.Gade@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Morgan, William &lt;William.Morgan@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Pasumarthi, Murali &lt;Murali.Pasumarthi@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Pirouzian, Ali &lt;Ali.Pirouzian@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Whipple, Richard &lt;Richard.Whipple@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Agahi, Sara &lt;Sara.Agahi@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Tubongbanua, Peejay &lt;Peejay.Tubongbanua@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Rasoulzadeh, Yasamin &lt;Yasamin.Rasoulzadeh@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Boghossian Ambrose, Nicole &lt;Nicole.Ambrose@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Isicson, Robin &lt;Robin.Isicson@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Marchesano, Charles &lt;Charles.Marchesano@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;
Cc: Aghassi, Sarah &lt;Sarah.Aghassi@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;; Lawson, Jennifer &lt;Jennifer.Lawson@sdcounty.ca.gov&gt;
Subject: Agenda and Goals for Nov 7 workshop at UCSD

We began the journey on the Regional Decarbonization Framework (RDF) last year in January when our Board of Supervisors directed us to work with UCSD on bringing the best available science to address a challenge that none of us can address in silos on our own &#8211; that of decarbonizing every major emitting sector in the region no matter where the emissions originate, for emissions know no boundaries.

As we are now transitioning from study to implementation, we are asking what can we do collectively in partnership with universities, business, labor and community, that will give us greater efficiencies in scale and speed to address climate change in the most effective and humane way possible. All of us from the County have sustainability embedded within the strategic direction of each of our departments. The goals of this workshop are to think about what more can we do? How do we implement sustainability within our core functions? How do we add value to our existing programs so that they also meet the goals of decarbonization? What new programs can achieve a better outcome?

We are meeting offsite at the Public Engagement Building at UCSD to visualize that greener future.

This workshop begins a new chapter in our relationship with UCSD as an academic partner to help us figure out how to implement this framework. This will be the first in a series of three workshops over our 3-year partnership. The UCSD team will focus on the implementation of the RDF for this workshop.  While of course there are many intersecting issues, the exercises will focus on land-based implications of RDF as well as simply getting everyone more proficient in the vocabulary and implications of the RDF itself. We will also walk through specific scenarios so that we leave the day with a practical understanding of how this framework can inform the work we do on a day-to-day basis in all our departments.

Thank you and looking forward to seeing you on Monday,

Murtaza and Sarah

-----Original Appointment-----
From: Lawson, Jennifer &lt;Jennifer.Lawson@sdcounty.ca.gov<mailto:Jennifer.Lawson@sdcounty.ca.gov>&gt;
Sent: Tuesday, October 18, 2022 5:12 PM
To: Lawson, Jennifer; Lawson, Jennifer; Cooper, Garrett; Dang, Ha; Alfaro, Suedy; Lintvedt, Inga; Sjoblom, Randall; Beaumont, Anne; Abbott, Jamie; Buonomo, Heather; Glueck, John-Ross; Harbert, Amy; Appel, Rebeca; Baxamusa, Murtaza; De La Rosa, Michael; Demorest, Erin; Hanson, Elise A; Acosta, Migell; Albright, Brian; Oakes, Chelsea; Salomon, Johanna; Trojanowska, Kasia; Bray, Kelly; Farmer, Tyler; Slovick, Mark; Talleh, Rami; Benham, Crystal; James, Kamilah; Gade, Derek; Morgan, William; Pasumarthi, Murali; Pirouzian, Ali; Whipple, Richard; Agahi, Sara; Tubongbanua, Peejay; Rasoulzadeh, Yasamin; Aghassi, Sarah; Boghossian Ambrose, Nicole; Isicson, Robin
Cc: Marchesano, Charles
Subject: All Day Offsite Workshop at UCSD
When: Monday, November 7, 2022 9:00 AM-4:00 PM (UTC-08:00) Pacific Time (US &amp; Canada).
Where: UCSD Social Sciences Public Engagement Building, 7th Floor Terrace

Please join us for an exciting new workshop led by our UCSD collaborators. This workshop would provide an initial convening of County of San Diego leadership and departments engaged in the implementation of decarbonization across municipal functions:  (1) Departmental Sustainability Planning, led by LUEG; (2) the Land Acquisition Roundtable, led by Parks &amp; Recreation; and (3) the renewable energy siting framework in the RDF.  This workshop would focus on the County departments&#8217; short/mid/long term goals towards sustainability; geospatial and policy relationships, conflicts, and potential synergies among and between land acquisition for park, conservation, and recreation purposes; and the extent of land area for new solar and wind energy required by alternative strategies in the RDF.  This approach would help build a common understanding of the policy levers and constraints for the departments in relationship to GHG reduction, and the distribution and extent of each set of land use needs, supporting the goal of identifying potential synergies including opportunities for multi-benefit uses of land for agriculture, water and watershed sustainability, recreation, and regional habitat linkage.

LOCATION: UCSD Social Sciences Public Engagement Building, 7th Floor Conference Room, in the new 6th College at UCSD, 9am - 4pm.
Location: https://goo.gl/maps/WzYiL8WjLEesmtyj8

Specific Parking Instructions will come soon.

From: Lynch, Dahvia <Dahvia.Lynch@sdcounty.ca.gov <mailto:Dahvia.Lynch@sdcounty.ca.gov> >
Sent: Tuesday, January 17, 2023 2:27 PM
To: Aghassi, Sarah <Sarah.Aghassi@sdcounty.ca.gov <mailto:Sarah.Aghassi@sdcounty.ca.gov> >
Cc: Demorest, Erin <Erin.Demorest@sdcounty.ca.gov <mailto:Erin.Demorest@sdcounty.ca.gov> >; Baxamusa, Murtaza <Murtaza.Baxamusa@sdcounty.ca.gov <mailto:Murtaza.Baxamusa@sdcounty.ca.gov> >
Subject: FW: Climate Action Research Grant - Letter of Intent (LOI) Due Jan 17th

Sarah-

My team met with the group described below on Friday (UCSD, SANDAG, Climate Action Campaign was added & UCSD spoke with the City of SD earlier so they did not join).  UCSD is looking for us to sign onto a Letter of Intent by COB today including providing bios and resumes of key staff.  The LOI is a precursor to be able to apply for the grant.   In short, there is much to be flushed out with this research effort before committing to join a grant application.  We don’t think there is application to the CAP but there may be for the Sustainable Land Use Framework, possibly RDF and other data analyses that the PODS could lead.  It is not clear if the ROI would be there for us.  This type of thing can easily balloon into a substantial work effort, which we would not be able to commit to with our current project responsibilities.  However, the City plans to join (I believe SANDAG does, too, but confirming) and we think it would be better to sign on than miss the opportunity, recognizing that the LOI is an initial formality and we can drop off prior to grant application if we determine the ROI doesn’t pencil.  We were very transparent with the group that it sounds like there are exciting possibilities here but we cannot commit until we know more.

Question by COB: Are you ok with us moving forward at this preliminary stage, recognizing we will need to flush out more details and reassess prior to joining on any formal application? (We can also give the BOS offices a heads up asap, if so.)

More detail about the grant project

*	Goal: Create a tool that uses existing data to help support decision-making

	*	Scenario planning tool
	*	Performance based approach
	*	Seeks to fill existing gaps in data analysis and layering (looking at the intersection of various existing data, etc.)
	*	To be designed based on what our needs are (i.e., parking policies, SB-9, relationship between built and natural environment to things like ghg, health and other impacts)
	*	(Not applicable to CAP because there are prescribed or highly vetted methodologies for the CAP work and new tools are not needed)

*	2 years up to $10M (likely 2-5M more than enough)
*	Outcomes- critical that the tool is used and the outcomes are the crux of the project

Ask/ County Obligation:

*	In-kind match- UCSD indicated this would not be a monetary match from us- no funding; some resources may even pass through UCSD and support our activities
*	Help shape proposal- collect data we have, participate in interactive working groups (outreach, other agencies)
*	Commit to ongoing use of research
*	Looking for suburban land use authority (other jurisdiction)

UCSD is requesting bios and resume for the County’s representatives by COB today.  We propose myself, Rami (for now- possibly swap with new PODs Deputy in the future), Murtaza, Don Chase (LRP Planning Manager).

Thank you.

Dahvia

 <http://www.sdcpds.org/>

Dahvia Lynch, AICP, Director

Erin, Marvin and Murtaza-  fyi

SANDAG reached out last week to connect us with Lawrence (Larry) Frank and Isaac Martin from UCSD (attached email).  They are putting together a research grant proposal for the California Climate Action Research program. This grant is through the UC system and requires institutional partners. Proposals must address California climate action priorities and result in actionable outcomes within 1-3 years. Awards will be from $2-10 million per proposal. SANDAG has agreed to be an institutional partner and they have indicated Heidi Von Blum, Planning Director for the City, is also onboard and coordinating with them.

I indicated we’d love to meet and learn more and we will be meeting with the collective group this Friday.

There is a near-term timeline in play- apparently the LOI is due next week (1/17).  The content and level of commitment that entails will be part of the discussion Friday.  We will find out more and brief it up before confirming our participation (may need to be via email or quick Teams chat given the short turnaround).

Murtaza- the Zoom info. for the meeting this Friday at 4pm is in the email below. Please let me know if you or anyone from the RDF team would like to join and I will let Larry know.

Thank you.

Dahvia

Subject: [External] Climate Action Research Grant - Letter of Intent (LOI) Due Jan 17th
Importance: High

Hi Everyone:

I hope you are all doing well and had a wonderful holiday season!  I am writing to provide an overview of the Climate Action Research Grant Program and the formation of a San Diego based team, and next steps.

Climate Action Research Grant Program

Just before the holidays in December, the state of California finally released the long awaited call for research (and action) proposals for projects that can demonstrate the ability to hit the ground running and sustain lasting practical and GHG mitigation, reduction and  impacts.  These will be 2 year projects ranging from around 500k up to $10 Million.  A letter of intent (LOI)  is due January 17th which is very soon for projects both with or without matching funds.

We are fortunate to have received a great deal of enthusiasm from all of the partners needed to generate a very competitive application.  The City of San Diego, San Diego County, SANDAG, the Climate Action Campaign, UCSD and other research partners have the ability to form a very strong team with complementary roles and skills that meet the requirements of this RFP.  Each organization has expressed an interest in working together to leverage our collective strengths, expertise, resources, and interests to create a strong and competitive LOI.  It is also possible that we may have the ability to show a significant matching funds by directly supporting other synergistic efforts underway in our region.  Foremost, I will soon be introducing the researchers who will be part of this effort – drawn from several research institutions.

We all share a collective interest and vision for reducing travel and place-based GHG emissions, providing affordable housing, and promoting public health, social justice, and safety through transit and active transportation investments combined with sustainable land use practices.  The policy and regulatory environment is rapidly changing with significant advancements at the state, regional, and local level taking place.  The immediate goal is to craft a compelling LOI that is sufficiently focused and targeted while also being general enough to be something we can all agree to quickly to get the green light from the state to move on to the proposal development stage where we will truly craft our work plan and what we want to accomplish.  There is a tremendous amount of work underway within each of our member organizations to generate a very unique and compelling project.

Next Steps

1.	Confirming who is on our team
2.	Getting your input - convening to discuss focus for LOI – stay tuned
3.	Drafting the AIMS, objectives, approach, and products
4.	Creating budget, timeline, and confirming match
5.	Completing letters of support
6.	Meeting all LOI requirements (see below)
7.	Filing LOI by the 17th

RFP Overview

The RFP can be found at this link: 2023 Request for Proposals (RFP) for the California Climate Action Research Initiative <https://urldefense.com/v3/__https:/ucop.us12.list-manage.com/track/click?u=2ae4678f6e94927e764ad58e5&id=2b1d6c9087&e=bdebfa5d99__;!!Mih3wA!C1EvLsrcFiLia4KrfJdlY70QxN39F1ZYvogi7XN4zYQMjSavP3C5PmbMvgHyowNMdVAe1s49NEnRFlYxhkb3QKU$> .  “The CA Climate Action Research Grants are intended to spur innovative applied research that addresses California’s climate goals, ensures that local communities are prepared and resilient, and prevents future disasters. This Request for Proposals (RFP) outlines two grant categories: CA Climate Action Seed Grants and CA Climate Action Matching Grants. Proposals for both grant categories must be synergistic with the State of California’s climate action priority or priorities. The California Climate Adaptation Strategy, in particular, presents a holistic vision for the state with the following outcome- based priorities:

1.	Strengthen protections for climate vulnerable communities
2.	Bolster public health and safety to protect against increasing climate risks
3.	Build a climate resilient economy
4.	Accelerate nature-based climate solutions and strengthen climate resilience of natural systems
5.	Make decisions based on the best available climate science
6.	Partner and collaborate to leverage resources

There are several goals and actions under each priority area. Together, this set of priorities requires the participation of multiple disciplines and different types of partners. There are also many other state resources and strategic plans that highlight agency-specific priorities. Appendix A provides a partial list of these resources.  An important emphasis of both grant programs is near-term outcome-based solutions for public impact. (See Sec. 2.4.) To that end, proposal teams must consider thoughtful partnerships, broadly defined based on the proposed project, that will lead to high-impact solutions informed by collective insights and experiences.

Letter of Intent

A letter of intent (LOI) is required for both grant categories. The Applicant PI must specify whether they are applying for a Seed Grant or Matching Grant. Then they must complete the appropriate application materials in the RGPO SmartSimple portal.

Letter of Intent Content. LOI submissions must include the following information:

*	Proposal title (100 characters or less)
*	Identification of Applicant PI and three-page biosketch/CV upload
*	Identification of Co-PIs, Co-Investigators/senior personnel, and other participating personnel

and/or organizations, institutions, or agencies, and a three-page biosketch/CV for each
o IdentifyatleastoneotherCaliforniaacademic/researchinstitutioncollaborator(requiredfor

Matching Grant; encouraged for Seed Grant).
o Identify at least one community/end-user partner (required for Matching Grant; encouraged for

Seed Grant). See Appendix B.

*	Specific California climate priority or priorities the research will address, with links to supporting

agency documents or reports (required). See Appendix A.

*	Actionable outcome(s) expected from the project
*	Abstract/Project Summary addressing the proposed scope of research and activities, rationale, and

potential impact and expected outcomes (2400 characters max)

*	Brief plans for required programmatic components, including collaboration plan, plan for engaging

communities and other end-users, and approach to leveraging available climate related resources,

or infrastructure.

*	Keywords: Research Categories and Identifiers to be selected from a drop-down menu.
*	Seed Grants only: Specific plan for leveraging the Seed Grant during or immediately after grant

period (specific grant or other funding source or opportunity)

*	Matching Grants only: Anticipated contributions for matching commitments (cash and/or in-kind).

See Appendix C.

Dr. Lawrence Frank, Professor

UC San Diego - Dept of Urban Studies and Planning

Social Sciences Public Engagement Building (PEB)

9625 Scholars Drive North MC 0517, PEB La Jolla, CA 92093

Honorary Healthy Cities Professor Faculty of Architecture Hong Kong University

Affiliate Professor in Population and Public Health University of British Columbia

Hi Stephanie,

Here is some language that might be helpful for your email to the Land Development Group. OSEJ would like to initiate most external contacts for the formal RWER, so the description below is more focused  the County’s technical tasks.  Since Max will already be in attendance he will be available to be the point person on this.

The County is currently evaluating the feasibility of storm drain diversions to water recycling facilities. As such, we are looking to map (via GIS data) local sewer networks and sewersheds draining to water recycling facilities (both direct potable reuse and recycled water [“purple pipe”]). We are also interested in mapping the service areas for all local direct potable reuse facilities, water recycling facilities, and desalination facilities. It would be very helpful if each representative could provide a publicly available data source or identify a subject matter expert (and provide contact information) for more information on obtaining this data.

We will most likely also need to reach out to specific water districts/agencies, but this could be a very helpful start!

Thanks,
Megan

### S4 · 24-409 · RE: RE: Lakeside Grand Opening Sat March 25th

`24-409~sandiego:24-409:33733195`

- **Listed because:** random spot check of an agreement
- **Origin:** produced for 24-409
- **Pre-label:** R (high) — Item 4 read literally: the thread says 'RDF/CAP are being asked to attend and table' and asks Ruiz 'Are you with RDF?'. It is sent to Elise Ruiz, the earlier message went to Murtaza Baxamusa, and it is dated 2023-03-07, inside the window.
- **Note:** The main topic is logistics for a library grand opening, but the RDF references and named correspondents meet item 4.

```text
Hi Elise,

Thank you for agreeing to attend the Lakeside Grand Opening.  It will be a lot of fun!  The event starts at 10, but you can get there anytime before to set up.  We will have offsite parking, but you should be able to pull into the parking lot off of Parkside to drop off any supplies first.  The Woodside entrance will be blocked for the tent.  Are you with RDF?  I just want to get my groups straight.

As for set up, we don’t have a ton of room because we are expecting a lot of customers that day.  We are trying to avoid having easels on the floor because they can become a tripping hazard.  You will, however, have a table to set up on and will have our lovely bioswale as a backdrop out the window (the flowers are blooming and it’s beautiful).  Please let me know if you have any questions and please send me your cell phone number in case we need to call you at the last minute.  Otherwise, we will send any other details through email.

Thanks!

Kristin Ward

Learning, Marketing, & Engagement

### S5 · 24-409 · Regional Water Equity - April 26th Planning

`24-409~sandiego:24-409:33733291`

- **Listed because:** random spot check of an agreement
- **Origin:** produced for 24-409
- **Pre-label:** R (high) — Item 3: Elise Ruiz on 2023-04-01 about the Regional Water Equity (RWER) April 26 Water Assembly kick-off. It also mentions UCSD's scope and UCSD-recommended presenters. Inside the window.
- **From:** Ruiz, Elise
- **To:** Barrett, Lara <Lara.Barrett@sdcounty.ca.gov>
- **Date:** 2023-04-01T11:48:54.724034-07:00 (window: in)
- **Attachments:** none
- **Original:** https://pra.sandiegocounty.gov/documents/33733291/download

```text
Hi Lara,

I’m sorry I didn’t have a chance to respond to your Teams message on Thursday – It was a packed day and I’ve been trying to manage the water project with a lot of changes lately.

First, thank you for working on the IRWM contact list, I will be presetting to the RAC this Wednesday, and it will be helpful to continue working with that group as things are moving fast.

The project has been a bit overwhelming, and we are working on a tight timeline to make the April 26th Water Assembly kick-off event come together. One thing that I brought up to Crystal and Stephanie is that we will need a speaker during this program to cover a technical piece concerning the drought status (UCSD was originally scoped for this work but has too much to cover with the literature review). I would like to check in with you to see if you may have any contacts who could fill this role and present a 10-15 minute presentation on April 26th? We would need all material/PPTs ready by April 14th, so I know this is a big ask.

Additionally, if you do not have any contacts, UCSD recommended these potential presenters:

  1.  Jeffrey Mount<https://www.ppic.org/person/jeffrey-mount/>, Public Policy Institute of California (first choice)

He could speak to the drought in terms of long-term trends, provide context for this year, and discuss what is on the horizon, first from a State perspective, but also identifying challenges specific to the RWER region.

  1.  Dr. F. Martin Ralph<https://mralph.scrippsprofiles.ucsd.edu/>, Director, Center for Western Weather Extremes<https://cw3e.ucsd.edu/>, Scripps Institute of Oceanography
Local technical expert on the physical process related to extremes in precipitation, climate projections, etc.

  1.  Luca Delle Monache<https://ldellemonache.scrippsprofiles.ucsd.edu/>, Deputy Director, Center for Western Weather Extremes, Scripps Institute of Oceanography
  2.  Tom Corringham, Research Economist, Center for Western Weather Extremes<https://cw3e.ucsd.edu/>, Scripps Institute of Oceanography

If you have time to secure a person either from this list or from your own recommendation, it would be really helpful to have your support with this task. I have mentioned this to Crystal and Stephanie, so if anyone can secure a presenter let’s keep each other posted.

Please feel free to give me a call this week if that’s easier, and thanks for all your effort on this project!

Elise Ruiz
She/her/ella<https://www.mypronouns.org/>
Group Program Manager
Land Use and Environment Group | County of San Diego
1600 Pacific Highway, Room 212, MS: A-6
San Diego, CA 92101
(619) 455-6865
```

Subject: Task - FAQs for Water Equity Report

Hi Chris,

I need some help developing a set of FAQs for the upcoming Water Assembly (outreach) for the Water Equity Report. It's ok to develop more questions that fewer, and copy existing FAQs we have developed, as we plan to refine the list during an upcoming meeting.

The attached "fact sheet" is draft (so isn't for distribution yet) but includes a few dot-points that can provide you some context for the type of information we'll share during the Water Assembly. The FAQs we discussed developing would be considered "low handing fruit" (not complex questions or in the weeds) and focus on questions we anticipate stakeholders may ask on local water needs, water equity, reliability, and supply including stormwater harvesting or capture/reuse.

We have FAQs on our website, and the IRWM program website www.sdirwmp.org <http://www.sdirwmp.org> ; these would be a good starting point. Of course, if you need further guidance, please reach out to me; we need a draft by Tuesday of next week (mid-day would be great).

Best,

Hi Elise and team,

The fraction of imported water supply for the County is around 70-80%. We would suggest the following multiple choice question, but are open to suggestions if alternate phrasing would make this more public-friendly or less “quiz-like”. I have also updated the poll question in the attached agenda.

What fraction of San Diego County’s water supply comes from outside of San Diego County?
   *   Less than 15%
   *   15-40%
   *   41-60%
   *   61-85%
   *   More than 85%

Thanks,
Megan

Megan M. Otto, PE (CA)

- **Attachments:** 0-RDF Playbook Action Addendum Based on Comments_4-28-23.docx

```text
Rimga:

Attached is the updated RDF Playbook Addendum. This reflects all comments submitted. Note that many actions appear in multiple scales (e.g., organization, community, region).

One key issue is how to refer to rural communities. We decided to use the term "disadvantaged communities" to address social equity concerns. In the draft document we say "disadvantaged communities and rural residents (or areas)." This may imply that rural areas are not necessarily "disadvantaged." Let us know if you have a preference on how to refer to these two populations.

Please let us know if you have any comments or questions.

Have a great weekend!

Thanks,
Scott

Director, Energy Policy Initiatives Center

San Diego, CA 92110

### S12 · 24-409 · Water Equity Project

`24-409~sandiego:24-409:33733272`

- **Listed because:** random spot check of an agreement
- **Origin:** produced for 24-409
- **Pre-label:** R (high) — Item 3/4: Roberta Leash on 2023-05-09 introduces Elise Ruiz for the Board-directed Water Equity Project, attaching the OSEJ RWER fact sheet. It relates to the RWER and is sent to Ruiz. Inside the window.
- **Note:** Attachment filename reads 'OSEJ_REWR_Fact Sheet.pdf' (sic), a transposition of RWER.
- **From:** "Leash, Roberta" <Roberta.Leash@sdcounty.ca.gov>
- **To:** "Tsoi; Samuel" <Samuel.Tsoi@sdcounty.ca.gov>; "Ruiz; Elise"	<Elise.Ruiz@sdcounty.ca.gov>
- **Date:** 2023-05-09T13:56:35-07:00 (window: in)
- **Attachments:** OSEJ_REWR_Fact Sheet.pdf
- **Original:** https://pra.sandiegocounty.gov/documents/33733272/download

```text
Good afternoon, Sam,

I want to introduce you to Elise Ruiz, our Group Program Manager in OSEJ. Elise is working on a big Water Equity Project directed by the Board of Supervisors, and I thought it would be great for the two of you to connect and discuss potential outreach opportunities and collaboration.

The project is focused on an assessment of water equity and sustainability through stormwater capturing in our diverse communities. I am assisting with tribal outreach, but I thought you might have connections with CBOs that could benefit from joining this initiative. To me, racial inequity leads us to conversations about water, housing, infrastructure, health, and environmental injustice. Elise is highly focused on engagement from all communities in the region.

Attached is brief fact sheet about the project but Elise could provide you more information about her goals for outreach and outcomes. Elise, if you would like me to participate in this conversation, please feel free to include me on the invite. ??

Thank you and have a lovely week,

Roberta Leash (She/Her/Hers)
Group Program Manager, County Tribal Liaison
Office of Sustainability and Environmental Justice<https://www.sandiegocounty.gov/osej/>
Land Use and Environment Group | County of San Diego
1600 Pacific Highway, Room 212, San Diego, CA 92101-2437
Mobile: 619-535-5786| Roberta.Leash@sdcounty.ca.gov
```

From: Daniel Serrano <Daniel@lesardevelopment.com <mailto:Daniel@lesardevelopment.com> >
Sent: Thursday, May 25, 2023 2:13 PM
To: Marcus Bush <mbush@maacproject.org <mailto:mbush@maacproject.org> >
Cc: Ruiz, Elise <Elise.Ruiz@sdcounty.ca.gov <mailto:Elise.Ruiz@sdcounty.ca.gov> >
Subject: RE: Regional Water Equity Report - Affordable Housing Connection

	Caution: This is an external email. Please take care when clicking links or opening attachments. When in doubt, contact your IT Department

Hi Marcus,

I hope you are well.  I’m working as a consultant with LeSar Development Consultants for the County of San Diego to identify ways in which to incentivize affordable housing to utilize sustainable use/reuse of water and stormwater.  This research is part of the County’s anticipated Regional Water Equity Report (the attached fact sheet provides additional context) that will help to inform the County’s larger drought management and water sustainability practices to proactively address the challenges of our region’s reliance on water sources.

We’d like to invite you and others from MAAC (e.g., Christopher Ramirez/Director of Real Estate Development) to participate in a one-hour (virtual) interview with LDC’s project team and County staff (Elise Ruiz/Group Program Manager with the County’s Land Use and Environment Group).  Our goal is to learn from your experiences in the affordable housing and stormwater capture and reuse fields which will help us to identify ways in which to incentivize affordable housing to utilize stormwater capture and reuse.  The interview would focus on stormwater infrastructure and affordable housing development process, the financial and regulatory barriers to implementing water and stormwater projects in housing, and potential opportunities to leverage better outcomes in both sectors.  Key themes from the interviews will be summarized in a brief memorandum to County staff.

Please let us know if you and Christopher (and others from your team) would be willing to join us for a one-hour virtual interview.

Thank you for your time and consideration.

Sincerely,

Danny Serrano, AICP

Daniel@lesardevelopment.com <mailto:Daniel@lesardevelopment.com>

619-850-1527

Subject: RE: Regional Water Equity Report - Affordable Housing Connection

	Caution: This is an external email. Please take care when clicking links or opening attachments. When in doubt, contact your IT Department

Hi Marcus,

Thanks again for volunteering to participate in a one-hour (virtual) interview with LDC staff, LDC’s sub-consultant (ARUP), and County staff.  Below is a list of meeting times for your consideration.  Our goal is to meet asap with your organization.  Please identify all times that may work for you. I’ve cc’d the folks you listed in your email below.  If necessary, I will send additional dates/times if none of these options work for your team.  I will circulate an agenda and send out a Teams meeting invite as soon as a meeting time has been identified.

*	Friday, June 9th at 4 PM
*	Tuesday, June 13th at 11 AM
*	Tuesday, June 13th at 4 PM
*	Tuesday, June 13th at 3 PM [Please note: this time is not preferred as there is currently a schedule conflict with County of San Diego staff]

Below is a preliminary list of questions that we are anticipating to ask during the interview.  These questions will help to guide our meeting.

*	We are exploring several water conservation, greywater reuse and stormwater reuse strategies for new and rehabilitation affordable housing projects in San Diego County. Have you implemented any of these strategies before?

	*	If so, what did you implement and how did you pay for it/them (e.g., programs, incentives, loans, tax credits etc.)?

	*	How effective were the measures (e.g., water savings, payback, operational performance successes/challenges)?

	*	What would you implement again? If not, why not?

	*	Is there more (efficiency / reuse measures) you would have done if more funding was available?

	*	Please describe, if applicable, how your organization has utilized LEED certification for different development types to help achieve water conservation, greywater reuse, and/or stormwater strategies for affordable housing projects. Do you have LEED accredited staff?

	*	Do you have year over year data on the projects’ water/sewer expenses before and after water conservation measures were implemented (if applicable)? If so, could you please share that data with us?

	*	Would you be willing to share any project proformas that include these water/stormwater strategies that you’ve implemented?

*	If you haven’t implemented any of these strategies before, please describe why.

	*	What were the major barriers (e.g., cost, technical expertise, regulatory hurdles etc.)? What would you need to implement these strategies?

	*	Do you think that it would be more feasible to implement these strategies in new construction or a substantial rehabilitation/retrofit project? Why?

Sincerely,

Danny Serrano, AICP

Daniel@lesardevelopment.com <mailto:Daniel@lesardevelopment.com>

619-850-1527

www.lesardevelopment.com <http://www.lesardevelopment.com/>

Sent: Monday, December 5, 2022 4:53 PM
To: Gaines, Stephanie <Stephanie.Gaines@sdcounty.ca.gov <mailto:Stephanie.Gaines@sdcounty.ca.gov> >
Cc: Mohrlock, Charles <Charles.Mohrlock@sdcounty.ca.gov <mailto:Charles.Mohrlock@sdcounty.ca.gov> >
Subject: RE: Onsite Treatment and Reuse of Non-potable Water

Hi Stephanie,

Below are my notes on the proposed regulations for Onsite Treatment and Reuse of Non-potable Water.

Regulatory Objective

To establish risk-based log reduction targets for the removal of pathogens such as enteric viruses, parasitic protozoa, and enteric bacteria for non-potable water sources (graywater, rainwater, stormwater, and blackwater), and non-potable end uses (toilet and urinal flushing, clothes washing, irrigation, and dust suppression).

Non-potable water sources

Non-potable end uses

*	Graywater
*	Rainwater
*	Stormwater
*	Blackwater

*	Toilet Flushing
*	Clothes Washing
*	Irrigation
*	Dust Suppression

Exclusions

Regulations do not apply to:

*	Untreated graywater systems that are used exclusively for subsurface irrigation
*	Untreated rainwater systems that are used exclusively for surface, subsurface, or drip irrigation
*	Municipal stormwater capture and use systems

What systems are subject to the new regulations?

An onsite treatment system is subject to the new regulations if:

*	It treats one or more of the following water sources, in whole or in part:

	*	Rainwater
	*	Stormwater
	*	Graywater, or
	*	Blackwater

*	The water sources are collected, treated, and used onsite
*	The treated water is used for non-potable purpose
*	It is connected to community sewer system as its only mean for disposal (blackwater system)

The regulations are intended for building-scale or district-scale (multiple buildings or campus) systems onsite reuse.

Risk-based Water Quality Standard

The goal is to achieve a (microbial) risk level of health protection from pathogens in onsite treated water of 1 in 10,000 infections per person per year. This is consistent with acceptable risk for EPA drinking water regulations and State of CA potable reuse regulations.

There will be two sets of log reduction targets for 1) indoor uses (toilet flushing, clothes washing) and 2) outdoor uses (irrigation, dust control, fire suppression, and car washing).

Schedule

The State Water Board is required to adopt the regulations by December 1, 2022. The Department of Housing and Community Development is required to develop and propose for adoption any necessary corresponding building standards to support the regulations adopted by the State Water Board by December 1, 2023.

The State Water Board’s website <https://www.waterboards.ca.gov/drinking_water/certlic/drinkingwater/onsite_nonpotable_reuse_regulations.html>  for the program offers no evidence that the regulation have been finalized. All procedural dates are listed as TBD.

Let me know if you have any questions you’d like me to look into.

Thanks,

Chris

From: Sandy Mathews <sandym@lwa.com <mailto:sandym@lwa.com> >
Sent: Tuesday, November 8, 2022 4:47 PM
To: Clapper, Kacen <kacen.clapper@wsp.com <mailto:kacen.clapper@wsp.com> >; Gaines, Stephanie <Stephanie.Gaines@sdcounty.ca.gov <mailto:Stephanie.Gaines@sdcounty.ca.gov> >
Cc: Ashli Desai <ashlid@lwa.com <mailto:ashlid@lwa.com> >
Subject: [External] Onsite Treatment and Reuse of Non-potable Water

Kacen, Stephanie,

Here is some follow-up information from the Policy and Permitting Subcommittee call. The Division of Drinking Water is developing regulations for the Onsite Treatment and Reuse of Non-potable Water. This is a legislative mandate. California Water Code section 13558 requires the State Water Board to adopt regulations on or before December 1, 2022. I just checked the website, and the schedule is still listed as TBD.

There was a workshop earlier this year and several stormwater representatives participated asking a lot of questions about whether this will affect our capture and use projects. The outcome was including the following statement in the FAQ, which is somewhat reassuring, however this might be something to track as the underlying language in the legislation and the draft information shared remains confusing.

12. Will the proposed regulations apply to municipal stormwater capture and use systems? No. The regulations are intended for building-scale or district-scale (multiple buildings or campus) systems onsite reuse. Watershed-scale stormwater capture projects designed to collect and store watershed-wide runoff during dry or wet weather conditions would not fit into the definition of OTNWS since the collection of the source water (stormwater) and in many cases, the use, are not onsite.

See more info here:  https://www.waterboards.ca.gov/drinking_water/certlic/drinkingwater/onsite_nonpotable_reuse_regulations.html <https://www.waterboards.ca.gov/drinking_water/certlic/drinkingwater/onsite_nonpotable_reuse_regulations.html>

Sandy

Good morning, Rebeca.

This sounds like a great opportunity for OSEJ!  I was thinking this would be a wonderful event for Eden to join if she were interested, but I see that she’s out through the 23rd.

I’d be happy to have one of our Sustainability staff join, but also wanted to reach out to you and LUEG.

We do provide solar fee waivers and previously provided green building fee waivers (that program has concluded). We are currently working on an affordable green building study that may lead to additional incentives.  I can provide more details and also reach out to Lulu on the key topics.

Question for you is if you’d like to join or have any representation from OSEJ?

Caroline, copying you here from the intergovernmental lens, as the IVLP is tied to the U.S. State Department.  We haven’t worked with them much and wasn’t sure if the County had a relationship with this entity.

Thank you.

Dahvia

From: Lulu Bonning <Lulu@sandiegodiplomacy.org <mailto:Lulu@sandiegodiplomacy.org> >
Sent: Friday, June 9, 2023 3:32 PM
To: Lynch, Dahvia <Dahvia.Lynch@sdcounty.ca.gov <mailto:Dahvia.Lynch@sdcounty.ca.gov> >
Subject: [External] Meeting Opportunity: Climate Action Delegation from Saudi Arabia

Good afternoon Dahvia,

I hope you are having a great week.

I am reaching out from the San Diego Diplomacy Council to see if you or another County representative is interested and available to meet with an incoming International Visitors Leadership Program <https://eca.state.gov/ivlp>  (IVLP) delegation we are hosting.

Through IVLP, the U.S. State Department's flagship professional exchange program, we will be hosting a delegation exploring "Approaches to Climate Change Challenges" later this month. Featuring 11 subject matter experts from Saudi Arabia, this delegation will be exploring:

*	Current policy frameworks of environmental laws, protections and conservation efforts
*	Strategies for reducing carbon emissions
*	Integrated approaches to develop climate resilient solutions which support vulnerable populations and maintain biodiversity

Please see bios attached for the visitors.

Would you be interested and available to meet with this delegation from 1:30 - 3:00pm on Wednesday, June 21?

During this meeting, we hope to discuss incentives provided by San Diego County to encourage sustainable building practices throughout the region, with a particular focus on remodels and renovations, and developer relations.

Let me know if you or one of your colleagues is available to meet with this delegation

Thank you very much - I look forward to hearing from you soon.

Warm regards,

Lulu Bonning

Nothing directly related comes to mind right now; Anali suggested our physical activity dice. Would a physical activity – emphasizing PA as part of a healthy lifestyle - at the table be appropriate or would you prefer more of a focus on food/nutrition? With the dice, the participant tosses both and then performs the amount and type of PA the up-facing side of the dice reflect.

Warm regards,

Barbara

Subject: FW: Earth Day

Good afternoon Elise and Anali,

First, I want to introduce you both to one another. Elise is in OSEJ with me and works as the project manager on the Regional Water Equity Report. Anali is a nutritionist in Public Health who works with tribal families on nutritional programming. Both of you have mentioned interest in attending the June 24th tribal Earth Day event at La Jolla so I wanted to coordinate a few items and maybe schedule a touch base meeting on June 21st at 10.a.m.? Would that work for you?

Attached is the event flier but here are some additional details to note and planning ideas ??

Event Details:

*	Arrive 9:30 a.m. to set up 1 shared table.
*	Event time: 11:00 a.m. -3:00 p.m.
*	Intertribal Earth Day with 8 surrounding tribes
*	Anticipate 600 tribal community guests
*	Anticipate 50 vendors from local agencies and tribes
*	Zero Waste event-
*	Pack lunch, sunscreen, and water (just in case!)
*	No cell service in the area
*	Pack own chairs

Supplies for Table:

*	Use LUEG tablecloth- Roberta
*	Roberta brings table and canopy
*	QR code for OSEJ website-Roberta
*	Water Equity information – Elise?
*	Fruits and freebies- Anali
*	Public Health QR Code to website/info- Anali
*	Maybe we can come up with an interactive activity for the table…?

Travel Arrangements:

*	I will likely travel from home as I live in Riverside County. I am not sure if they two of you would be interested in carpooling but maybe its an option. An OERJ team member may also tag along but I will find out early next week.
*	Cell service is bad once you pass the round about by Rincon off HWY 76. The event is on the same turn off as the La Jolla Trading Post (gas station/casino). There are campground signs to take you below the gas station, but they are faded. ??

Thank you both for your support. It should be great weather!

Roberta Leash, Group Program Manager, County Tribal Liaison

Office of Sustainability and Environmental Justice <https://www.sandiegocounty.gov/osej/>

County of San Diego Land Use and Environment Group

Digital Equity Survey Promotion

The digital equity survey will help identify internet connection barriers and needs. It can be taken online and is accessible in 14 different languages. There’s an opportunity to run a speed test which will be used to help the State better understand internet speed landscape in the San Diego region! Below are links to the public survey and an outreach toolkit to help promote it.

*	Digital Equity Survey <https://lnks.gd/l/eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiJ9.eyJidWxsZXRpbl9saW5rX2lkIjoxMTMsInVyaSI6ImJwMjpjbGljayIsInVybCI6Imh0dHA6Ly9iaXQubHkvQ0FEaWdpdGFsRXF1aXR5U3VydmV5IiwiYnVsbGV0aW5faWQiOiIyMDIzMDYxMy43ODE3MDcwMSJ9.8lDX9LimE1y3aqEZrGbSAaQwexPT9bjxRD8D2JGoRF8/s/2172234685/br/205383183870-l>

### S28 · 24-409 · Stoney Oak Ranch Support

`24-409~sandiego:25-3152:48517353`

- **Listed because:** random spot check of an agreement
- **Origin:** other production (25-3152)
- **Pre-label:** N (high) — Public comment supporting Stoney Oaks Ranch before a planning group meeting, dated 2024-04-03. Outside the window, with no request terms.
- **From:** Vittoria Allen <vittoriavincenza@gmail.com>
- **To:** travislyonacpg@gmail.com
- **Date:** 2024-04-03T15:46:46-07:00 (window: out)
- **Attachments:** none
- **Original:** https://pra.sandiegocounty.gov/documents/48517353/download

```text
I’m writing in support of Stoney Oaks Ranch ahead of the community planning group meeting tomorrow.

Stoney Oaks Ranch has been a little slice of heaven for my two girls. Almost 3 years ago, for my oldest’s 5th birthday we reached out about hosting our party there and she has been hooked ever since.

We have attended their once a week camps, summer camps, and take horse riding lessons as well.

My girls have learned skills they wouldn’t have learned had they not had people like Angie and Krystal leading the way to teach kids from a young age a deep appreciation for being outdoors, working with your hands, and caring for animals. Kids don’t just get to show up and play. They work hard and they learn. They learn about respect, and responsibility, how to work as a team, and even self confidence. And when it’s time to go home, they don’t want to leave because they are where children should be: outside, climbing in a treehouse, getting dirty, and exploring.

We live in a small home with a small yard and what my girls get to experience at Stoney Oaks is unique and something they can’t get by just walking into our own backyard. Certainly not the joy and therapeutic wonder of being around horses and other farm animals!

We’re so grateful for everyone at Stoney Oaks Ranch and our weekly schedule has felt empty without my kids being able to gather with other kids who share the same love of being outside and caring for animals.

We LOVE Stoney Oaks and give our full whole hearted support to everything they do.

Sincerely,
Vittoria Allen
```

- **From:** "Cox, Justin" <Justin.Cox@10news.com>
- **To:** "travislyonacpg@gmail.com" <travislyonacpg@gmail.com>, "rsaldano@contelproject.com" <rsaldano@contelproject.com>

```text
Good morning Travis and Richard,

My name is Justin Cox, and I am an assignment editor with ABC 10News. I wanted to flag this email my colleague Athena left last night. If you have any information you can share about the proposed battery storage facility in Alpine, feel free to let us know.

Thank you,
Justin Cox

From: Jreij, Athena <Athena.Jreij@10news.com>
Sent: Thursday, September 26, 2024 9:45 PM
To: travislyonacpg@gmail.com; rsaldano@contelproject.com
Cc: Cox, Justin <Justin.Cox@10news.com>; Montano, Rachel <Rachel.Montano@10news.com>
Subject: ABC 10NEWS INQUIRY: Alpine Battery Storage Concerns

Hello Travis and Richard,

My name is Athena Jreij I work with ABC 10News. I hope you're doing well. I'm reaching out because we saw residents of Alpine discussing a proposed battery storage facility to be placed in the city and that this issue was discussed at the Alpine Community Planning Group's meeting tonight.

Can you share what the next step for this facility is and what action was taken at tonight's meeting? Did residents give public comment against the facility?

Thank you,
Athena

Athena Jreij

Assignment Editor

ABC 10News

Athena.Jreij@10news.com<mailto:Athena.Jreij@10news.com>

4600 Air Way | San Diego, CA 92102

```text
Good evening,

The board of supervisors meeting tomorrow has BESS development design and
safety standards on the agenda, item #30.

Here is the link to the main Board of Supervisors Meeting
Agenda/links/comment instructions -
https://www.sandiegocounty.gov/content/sdc/cob/bosa.html

Here is the link to the e-comments for that specific agenda item. -
https://sdcounty.granicusideas.com/meetings/2087-board-of-supervisors/agenda_items/6750ca96f395e78dfb00594b-30-new-requirements-for-battery-energy-storage-sy

Here is a summary of comments that you can cut and paste that echo Mary
Davis's comments and have a link to the pdf.

1. Integrate State Fire-Severity Hazard Maps
2. Utilize Consistent Land-Use Standards (to match 'Fire Safe' C.A.P.)
3. Limit Projects per capita or per Square Miles
4. Require Plume Modeling as Determinant
5. Require $10 liability bond
6. Require 2 points of egress for ALL projects
7. Require projects to be vaulted underground

See full list of recommendations at: https://bit.ly/3ZfJijS

I support BESS safety standards but your guidelines do not go far enough.
Attached is the PDF summary of the recommendation sheet Mary Davis
developed.

*Travis Lyon* | Chair
Alpine Community Planning Group
P.O. Box 1419, Alpine, CA 91903-1419
619.952.8607

- **Cc:** "Carrillo-Hough, Kamden J" <KamdenJ.CarrilloHough@sdcounty.ca.gov>
- **Date:** 2025-01-31T19:35:48+00:00 (window: out)
- **Attachments:** PDS2025-STP-25-003-PDS-PLN-346.pdf, PDS2025-STP-25-003-PDS-PLN-346S.pdf, PDS2025-STP-25-003-PDS-PLN-366.pdf, PDS2025-STP-25-003-PDS-PLN-399F.pdf, PDS2025-STP-25-003-PDS-PLN-399S.pdf, PDS2025-STP-25-003-PDS-PLN-399W.pdf, PDS2025-STP-25-003-PDS-PLN-524.pdf, PDS2025-STP-25-003-PDS-PLN-534.pdf, PDS2025-STP-25-003-PDS-PLN-571.pdf, PDS2025-STP-25-003-PDS-PLN-Preliminary construction drawings 1-9-25.pdf
- **Original:** https://pra.sandiegocounty.gov/documents/48517361/download

```text
Hello,

Attached are documents pertaining to a Discretionary Permit for a Site Plan (PDS2025-STP-25-003).  If you normally receive a plot plan, it will be delivered through regular mail within the next few days.

Pursuant to Board Policy I-1,
(https://www.sandiegocounty.gov/content/dam/sdc/cob/docs/policy/I-1.pdf), this request for a recommendation is being sent to you because a discretionary permit application has been submitted to Planning & Development Services (PDS) for a project within your Community/Design Review Area.  Once a recommendation is made, please forward it to PDS with Form #534 so the recommendation can be included in correspondence to the project applicant and as part of the staff report for the appropriate hearing body of officer.

Every effort should be made to submit a group recommendation within seven calendar days following the CPG/DRB/CSG meeting.

If you have any questions or comments regarding PDS2025-STP-25-003, please reach out to Kamden J. Carrillo-Hough directly at KamdenJ.CarrilloHough@sdcounty.ca.gov. If for any reason the project is reassigned, Kamden J. Carrillo-Hough will reach out and provide an updated point of contact for the project.

Sincerely,

[cid:image001.png@01DB73D4.30951820]
Alexander Shilvock, Land Use Aide
Planning & Development Services
Zoning Division
5510 Overland Avenue

Second Opinion: A second opinion is available on any interpretation
or correction provided and will not affect the level of customer service
you receive. We ask you to provide your interpretation of the code along
with any health and safety concerns that the interpretation may cause
and how this will affect your project. The second opinion will come
from one of our senior staff or a supervisor who will have a chance to
take a deeper look at supporting documentation on both sides of the issue.
```

### S40 · 24-409 · [External] Colorado Billionaire Shouldn’t Decide Our Safety - Say No to HGVS

`24-409~sandiego:25-4697:52664360`

- **Listed because:** random spot check of an agreement
- **Origin:** other production (25-4697)
- **Pre-label:** N (high) — Resident public comment to the Board of Supervisors, dated 2025-09-30, opposing Harmony Grove Village South. It is outside the date window and has no request terms or listed correspondents.
- **From:** Greg Kaczur <gregkaczur@aol.com>
- **To:** terra.lawson-remer@sdcounty.ca.gov; joel.anderson@sdcounty.ca.gov; monica.montgomerysteppe@sdcounty.ca.gov; Jim.Desmond@sdcounty.ca.gov; PA@sdcounty.ca.gov; Andrew.Potter@sdcounty.ca.gov; PublicComment@sdcounty.ca.gov; Paul.Worlie@sdcounty.ca.gov; Kyle.LaVelle@sdcounty.ca.gov; Andrew.Hayes@sdcounty.ca.gov; Jeffrey.Yuen@sdcounty.ca.gov; Eric.Henson@sdcounty.ca.gov; Hunter.McDonald@sdcounty.ca.gov; Dahvia.Lynch@sdcounty.ca.gov; amy.harbert@sdcounty.ca.gov; Mark.Slovick@sdcounty.ca.gov; efhgtc@gmail.com; Bianca.Lorenzana@sdcounty.ca.gov; Montagne@rsf-fire.org; mcquead@rsf-fire.org; ashcraft@rsf-fire.org; hillgren@rsf-fire.org; malin@rsf-fire.org; stine@rsf-fire.org; Vince.Nicoletti@sdcounty.ca.gov
- **Date:** 2025-09-30T17:55:48-07:00 (window: out)
- **Attachments:** none
- **Original:** https://pra.sandiegocounty.gov/documents/52664360/download

```text
Dear Chair Lawson-Remer, board members and staff:

My family and myself have lived in Harmony Grove for 40 years. I’m a retired firefighter/ captain with over 30 years of experience. We’ve been through multiple big fires. It’s just a matter of time before another event happens. During the 2014 Cocos Fire, the wind changed directions and about 30 homes burned, and it took us almost two hours just to get a few miles out. Back then, the village site was just dirt, and even that helped slow the flames. Roads were jammed even with far fewer cars.

Now Harmony Grove Village is built (the community actually testified in favor of the project back in 2011). We love our new neighbors, but evacuations are going to be seriously constricted: so many vehicles trying to get out on the same narrow roads. Years ago the County told us the safe place for growth was north of the creek, near the old egg ranch which is why we supported HGV. South of the creek was a dead-end fire-prone bowl unless there’s a second way out. County even removed a proposed road to Del Dios Highway from the road plans. It’s the main reason none of us can get insurance – we’re surrounded by brush and no way out.

HGV South would add another 1000 cars onto the same routes that were gridlocked back in 2014. And to make things worse, the County felt it was ok to give a Colorado billionaire a waiver for secondary access in a bowl that has repeatedly caught fire throughout the years. The County never modeled a full community evacuation (even though we asked repeatedly); they looked only at the immediate project area. We had to fundraise and pay for an independent expert, and the verdict was sobering: entrapment is likely. His analysis showed that it would take seven hours or more to evacuate everyone, but fires arrive much sooner.

Our fire chief also recently acknowledged HGV South wouldn’t pass today’s standards without a secondary access. Please vote no on HGV South, or at least require real secondary access. Our safety has to come first.

Respectfully,
Greg Kaczur
9033 Harmony Grove Rd

Sent from my iPhone
```

Sent: Wednesday, July 27, 2022 2:33 PM
To: Amy Lerner &lt;amlerner@ucsd.edu<mailto:amlerner@ucsd.edu>&gt;; Juli Beth Hinds &lt;jhinds@ucsd.edu<mailto:jhinds@ucsd.edu>&gt;; Gordon McCord &lt;gmccord@ucsd.edu<mailto:gmccord@ucsd.edu>&gt;
Cc: Hanson, Elise A &lt;EliseA.Hanson@sdcounty.ca.gov<mailto:EliseA.Hanson@sdcounty.ca.gov>&gt;; Appel, Rebeca &lt;Rebeca.Appel@sdcounty.ca.gov<mailto:Rebeca.Appel@sdcounty.ca.gov>&gt;
Subject: UCSD workshops

Dear UCSD team,

The approach on the following workshops has been approved. The next steps for the UCSD team are:

Workshop #1: Please place a hold on November 7 on all your calendars and reserve the PEB space. It would be helpful to begin drafting an agenda/program that we would be discuss with Sarah on September 2.
Workshop #2: We will be coordinating with the Planning and Development Services Department and get back to you.
Workshop #3: It would be helpful if we can develop a timeline. We will be coordinating with our Vice Chair Nora Vargas.

Thank you!
Murtaza
____________________________

  1.  Enterprise-wide Alignment of RDF
Date: November 7, 2022 (back-up would be November 4, 2022)
Participants:  County of San Diego internal department leadership, internal stakeholders
Duration: Full-day 2-part workshop
Location: 7th floor terrace at the Public Engagement Building

This workshop would provide an initial convening of County of San Diego leadership and departments engaged in the implementation of decarbonization across municipal functions:  (1) Departmental Sustainability Planning, led by LUEG; (2) the Land Acquisition Roundtable, led by Parks &amp; Recreation; and (3) the renewable energy siting framework in the RDF.  This workshop would focus on the County departments&#8217; short/mid/long term goals towards sustainability; geospatial and policy relationships, conflicts, and potential synergies among and between land acquisition for park, conservation, and recreation purposes; and the extent of land area for new solar and wind energy required by alternative strategies in the RDF.  This approach would help build a common understanding of the policy levers and constraints for the departments in relationship to GHG reduction, and the distribution and extent of each set of land use needs, supporting the goal of identifying potential synergies including opportunities for multi-benefit uses of land for agriculture, water and watershed sustainability, recreation, and regional habitat linkage.

***This workshop would be in tandem with the preparation of document defining the scope of work regarding the team&#8217;s advisory role for LUEG and the RDF. [Ref: Tasks 2.1 &amp; 2.2]

  2.  Sustainable Land Use Framework (SLUF)
Timeline: Early 2023
Participants: County&#8217;s Planning &amp; Development Services Department, external stakeholders
Location: First floor lab on the ground floor of the Public Engagement Building

This workshop will focus on the themes adopted by the Board in September 2022 in the context of the General Plan for the unincorporated area of the County. It would not be framed as RDF implementation but would examine the potential alignments of SLUF with decarbonization pathways. These themes could include: (1) decarbonization of buildings, communities, and transportation through policies and land use; (2) increase opportunities for sustainable and resilient development and housing for all; (3) support green economies, innovative infrastructure, agriculture, and sustainable best practices; (4) conserve and protect existing natural habitats and resources, and increase access to open and green spaces; and (5) prioritize social equity and environmental justice in sustainable land use policies, programs, and plans.

  3.  Binational Regional Climate Action Plan
Timeframe:  Last quarter 2023/first quarter 2024
Participants:  Jurisdictions in San Diego County with CAPs (municipalities; SANDAG; Unified Port of San Diego; US Department of Defense); California Office of Policy &amp; Research
Invited Guests:  Governments of Baja California Norte; City of Tijuana; UABC

The third workshop would engage stakeholders across the county and San Diego-Tijuana region in evaluating the consistency and commonality of purpose and strategies among and between the individual Climate Action Plans (CAPs) adopted pursuant to State directives and local initiative.  UCSD students would prepare assessments of each plan in a unified framework illustrating the baselines, policies, action steps, and reporting metrics across the region.  The workshop will focus on building common awareness and purpose; exchanging strategies and ideas; identifying areas of potential re-alignment; and building a binational baseline for emissions and measures. This workshop would explore the possibility of a Binational Regional Climate Action Plan.

**UCSD also could extend some of the mapping into Baja California to illustrate trans-border issues, notably for transportation, housing, watersheds, and habitat.

_____________________________________
Murtaza H. Baxamusa, PhD, AICP
Program Manager for Regional Sustainability
Land Use and Environment Group
County of San Diego
```

Subject: [External] Data Request for Land Development Workgroup

Hi Stephanie,

Here is some language that might be helpful for your email to the Land Development Group. OSEJ would like to initiate most external contacts for the formal RWER, so the description below is more focused  the County’s technical tasks.  Since Max will already be in attendance he will be available to be the point person on this.

The County is currently evaluating the feasibility of storm drain diversions to water recycling facilities. As such, we are looking to map (via GIS data) local sewer networks and sewersheds draining to water recycling facilities (both direct potable reuse and recycled water [“purple pipe”]). We are also interested in mapping the service areas for all local direct potable reuse facilities, water recycling facilities, and desalination facilities. It would be very helpful if each representative could provide a publicly available data source or identify a subject matter expert (and provide contact information) for more information on obtaining this data.

We will most likely also need to reach out to specific water districts/agencies, but this could be a very helpful start!

Thanks,
Megan

Subject: Task - FAQs for Water Equity Report

Hi Chris,

I need some help developing a set of FAQs for the upcoming Water Assembly (outreach) for the Water Equity Report. It's ok to develop more questions that fewer, and copy existing FAQs we have developed, as we plan to refine the list during an upcoming meeting.

The attached "fact sheet" is draft (so isn't for distribution yet) but includes a few dot-points that can provide you some context for the type of information we'll share during the Water Assembly. The FAQs we discussed developing would be considered "low handing fruit" (not complex questions or in the weeds) and focus on questions we anticipate stakeholders may ask on local water needs, water equity, reliability, and supply including stormwater harvesting or capture/reuse.

We have FAQs on our website, and the IRWM program website www.sdirwmp.org <http://www.sdirwmp.org> ; these would be a good starting point. Of course, if you need further guidance, please reach out to me; we need a draft by Tuesday of next week (mid-day would be great).

Best,
