```text
Subject: RE: Onsite Treatment and Reuse of Non-potable Water

Hi Stephanie,

Below are my notes on the proposed regulations for Onsite Treatment and Reuse of Non-potable Water.

Regulatory Objective

To establish risk-based log reduction targets for the removal of pathogens such as enteric viruses, parasitic protozoa, and enteric bacteria for non-potable water sources (graywater, rainwater, stormwater, and blackwater), and non-potable end uses (toilet and urinal flushing, clothes washing, irrigation, and dust suppression).

Non-potable water sources

Non-potable end uses

*	Graywater
*	Rainwater
*	Stormwater
*	Blackwater

*	Toilet Flushing
*	Clothes Washing
*	Irrigation
*	Dust Suppression

Exclusions

Regulations do not apply to:

*	Untreated graywater systems that are used exclusively for subsurface irrigation
*	Untreated rainwater systems that are used exclusively for surface, subsurface, or drip irrigation
*	Municipal stormwater capture and use systems

What systems are subject to the new regulations?

An onsite treatment system is subject to the new regulations if:

*	It treats one or more of the following water sources, in whole or in part:

	*	Rainwater
	*	Stormwater
	*	Graywater, or
	*	Blackwater

*	The water sources are collected, treated, and used onsite
*	The treated water is used for non-potable purpose
*	It is connected to community sewer system as its only mean for disposal (blackwater system)

The regulations are intended for building-scale or district-scale (multiple buildings or campus) systems onsite reuse.

Risk-based Water Quality Standard

The goal is to achieve a (microbial) risk level of health protection from pathogens in onsite treated water of 1 in 10,000 infections per person per year. This is consistent with acceptable risk for EPA drinking water regulations and State of CA potable reuse regulations.

There will be two sets of log reduction targets for 1) indoor uses (toilet flushing, clothes washing) and 2) outdoor uses (irrigation, dust control, fire suppression, and car washing).

Schedule

The State Water Board is required to adopt the regulations by December 1, 2022. The Department of Housing and Community Development is required to develop and propose for adoption any necessary corresponding building standards to support the regulations adopted by the State Water Board by December 1, 2023.

The State Water Board’s website <https://www.waterboards.ca.gov/drinking_water/certlic/drinkingwater/onsite_nonpotable_reuse_regulations.html>  for the program offers no evidence that the regulation have been finalized. All procedural dates are listed as TBD.

Let me know if you have any questions you’d like me to look into.

Thanks,

Chris

Sent: Tuesday, November 8, 2022 4:47 PM
To: Clapper, Kacen <kacen.clapper@wsp.com <mailto:kacen.clapper@wsp.com> >; Gaines, Stephanie <Stephanie.Gaines@sdcounty.ca.gov <mailto:Stephanie.Gaines@sdcounty.ca.gov> >
Cc: Ashli Desai <ashlid@lwa.com <mailto:ashlid@lwa.com> >
Subject: [External] Onsite Treatment and Reuse of Non-potable Water

Kacen, Stephanie,

Here is some follow-up information from the Policy and Permitting Subcommittee call. The Division of Drinking Water is developing regulations for the Onsite Treatment and Reuse of Non-potable Water. This is a legislative mandate. California Water Code section 13558 requires the State Water Board to adopt regulations on or before December 1, 2022. I just checked the website, and the schedule is still listed as TBD.

There was a workshop earlier this year and several stormwater representatives participated asking a lot of questions about whether this will affect our capture and use projects. The outcome was including the following statement in the FAQ, which is somewhat reassuring, however this might be something to track as the underlying language in the legislation and the draft information shared remains confusing.

12. Will the proposed regulations apply to municipal stormwater capture and use systems? No. The regulations are intended for building-scale or district-scale (multiple buildings or campus) systems onsite reuse. Watershed-scale stormwater capture projects designed to collect and store watershed-wide runoff during dry or wet weather conditions would not fit into the definition of OTNWS since the collection of the source water (stormwater) and in many cases, the use, are not onsite.

See more info here:  https://www.waterboards.ca.gov/drinking_water/certlic/drinkingwater/onsite_nonpotable_reuse_regulations.html <https://www.waterboards.ca.gov/drinking_water/certlic/drinkingwater/onsite_nonpotable_reuse_regulations.html>

Sandy
```

```text
Hi Travis! I’m a TV reporter for Spectrum News. The station is based in LA, but I live in and cover San Diego County for them! I’m working on a TV story on the Alpine County Park and the lawsuit that was just filed to stop it. I have an interview with someone on-camera who is against the park, but I am trying to find someone to interview on-camera who can talk about why they support it, so the story is balanced! I was chatting with the County, and since they are not allowed to comment on pending litigation, they thought you might be a good option for an interview since you spoke in favor of the project! If you are willing, we would just meet at the site and look around and talk about why it would be good for the community and why you support it! I shouldn’t need more than half hour or so, and this would be an on-camera pre-recorded interview that we would schedule sometime in February!

--
Bree Steffen
```

### S63 · 25-3152 · Alpine Community Planning Group Chair Walks like A Duck.

`25-3152~sandiego:25-3152:48518147`

- **Listed because:** random spot check of an agreement
- **Origin:** produced for 25-3152
- **Pre-label:** R (high) — Item 4. Russell Walsh, a member of the public, emailed Chair Lyon on 2024-07-31 about the ACPG Loveland letters of support, inside the window.
- **Note:** Hostile, accusatory content, which does not affect responsiveness.
- **From:** Russell Walsh <russellwalsh2003@yahoo.com>
- **To:** Travis Lyon <travislyonacpg@gmail.com>
- **Date:** 2024-07-31T01:21:26+00:00 (window: in)
- **Attachments:** 1722387437193blob.jpg
- **Original:** https://pra.sandiegocounty.gov/documents/48518147/download

```text
Travis Lyon

Chair

Alpine Community Planning Group

With blind CC's

Hello,Travis , 

I am concerned about your refusal to write the letters of support from three months ago.   Supervisor Anderson has already given them and the massive overwhelming interest in sunrise to sunset  has been demonstrated many times before your own eyes, ears, heart and mind. 

 

In fact, anyone acting like you have, independently canceling many full ACPG votes since I started going advocating at ACPG meetings in 2022, with inaction on the motion passed and the devastating consequences,   would logically be considered a person with a terrible self-serving conflict of interest. 

What would start to change my mind is the letters of support that everybody in your group, including yourself,  and  except  Darlene Cossio of Friends of Loveland voted for. Did you make a deal with Darlene or somebody else to shelf the motion post vote? Is shelving things by yourself for whatever reason part of the ACPG’s foundation? 

Why do you think those letters of support would not benefit the grass roots Loveland Defenders and Advocates who solicited them? The community including the board members who voted for them. Supervisor Anderson’s constituents? Have you performed such dedicated service to Loveland? Do you fancy yourself  working Loveland Miracles for the community behind the scenes? You never did before now. 

I have a theory of what you are up to but would not like to expend the energy to confirm them yet. However, I will say this. I imagine you like to console yourself that I am rude without concern for your own rudeness as exemplified in canceling unanimous or near unanimous ACPG votes even though you leave the meetings , time and time again, with promises to fulfill them. Does the board not monitor you for these failures? What is legitimate about your leadership and defaulting on board votes? 

Please write and send the letters of support ASAP, as promised months ago,  with proof of service to deserving advocates. The timing of now is really important. 

Russell
```

```text
Good afternoon,

Please see the attached information on the candidate filing period that is now open.  If you are receiving this email your term expires at the end of this year and you will need to file for re-election if you would like to continue on the Planning Group.

The filing has to be completed in person at the county ROV.  The deadline to complete the paperwork is August 9, 2024, but my advice is to get it completed this week or next week because the closer we get to the deadline the longer it takes to process at the ROV because the last few days there are a rush of candidates who wait till the deadline.
```

```text
On Sun, Oct 27, 2024 at 8:08 PM APG <associationofplanninggroups@gmail.com>
wrote:

> Good Evening APG Members,
>
> Please note our next APG meeting will be in-person on Saturday, January
> 4th, 2025, from 10:00am to noon at the Lakeside Library.
>
> *APG Announcement to SANDAG Board on Oct. 25th:*
> We have good news. The second wildfire and emergency evacuation tour was
> held last Friday for SR-94. Organizing the tour were SANDAG's Ariana Galvan
> and Jamul-Dulzura Community Planning Group member, Paul Dombkowski. In
> addition to several members from the Planning Group and SANDAG there were
> participants from Caltrans, San Diego County Sheriff's office, and CalFire,
> including Battalion Chief, Sam Friedman. The unincorporated communities
> value the partnership and collaboration these tours create as we work
> toward a safer travel experience for all. Thank you.
>
> *SANDAG CEO, Mario Orso Report on Oct. 11th:*
> -Mario Orso said that they were cooperating with the Independent Auditor
> and working to build openness and trust.
> -Regarding taking the 125 out of the Regional Plan as a toll road - would
> we be in compliance with CARB (California Air Resources Board). We would
> barely be meeting our GHG emissions and VMT measure.
> -Otay Mesa East has 65% of the design completed.
>
> APG comment to Board: Mario, this is October, wildfire season in the rural
> areas. What have we done to alleviate the bottleneck issues with the
> highways that are used for evacuation heading west? There are bottlenecks
> that have been there forever and we've highlighted those as being potential
> fatal zones for evacuees. Where are we with resolving bottleneck issues
> with lanes that are one lane, two, three, that are continuing to change as
> people evacuate west? Thank you. (32:00/ Oct. 11)
>
> SANDAG reponse (Mario Orso): What route did you mention?
>
> APG response: This would be specifically 67, but there are bottlenecks on
> other evacuation routes.
>
> SANDAG response (Mario Orso): Everett, do you want to help me on what's
> the emergency evacuation plan from Caltrans?
>
> Caltrans response (Everett Townsend): Yeah, so Caltrans has been working
> with emergency responders, CalFire, CHP and others to reassess on an annual
> basis, is what we do to reassess our highway system. And, to identify where
> additional improvements may be made. Over the last few years, Caltrans has
> completed projects there that create some widening and create opportunities
> to improve traffic flow through there. Most of those issues that state
> route 67 project is at some point will be presented here at the Board, but
> you know, just to be informed that Caltrans is well aware of the concerns
> of the communities in Ramona and further east. As well as CHP and CalFire.
> And, we certainly have a plan to address any issues that may come up during
> this time.
>
> *State Route 125 Toll Removal Oct. 11th and 25th:*
> Oct. 11th, Mario Orso, reported that a pre-environmental analysis and an
> equity analysis need to be performed and these two actions will take one
> year.
>
> Vivian Moreno, City of San Diego comment: I continue to be extremely
> disappointed with how SANDAG is handling the 125. We're the only community
> (South Bay Area) that gets tripled taxed on this toll. We pay taxes every
> single year. We pay the toll and we pay Transnet funding. We're the only
> community that does this. This seems to me like we are slow walking this
> issue.... How much longer do we have to wait? 2042?
>
> Oct. 25th Courtney Ruby, SANDAG Independent Performance Auditor, reported
> that the Executive Management Team which included former CEO, Hasan
> Ikhrata; were aware of and actively working to resolve major issues with
> the 125 Tolling System, yet failed to notify the Board of this until
> October of 2023. Fully 18 months passed before the Board was informed of
> these significant issues impacting residents of the South Bay Region. A
> single-source contract was presented and approved by the Board in January
> of 2024 based on the Boards belief that they could rely on the
> recommendations of Executive Management. As it turned out this contract
> between SANDAG and Deloitte to manage the 125 Toll Road systems was
> deficient and absent an exchange of basic system requirements.
>
> Ed Musgrove, City of San Marcos comment: We were lied to by the Executive
> Team. Until the names of those in charge are revealed I have no confidence.
>
> *Wildfire Evacuation Tour SR-94:*
> On Friday, October 18th members of SANDAG, Caltrans and the Janual
> Community Planning Group completed an evacuation tour. Please see Paul
> Dombkowski's report sent in a previous email on October 23rd.
>
> *News:*
> At the October 11th and 25th SANDAG Board meetings we had zero voting
> representation from anyone living in the Unincorporated Area. Only Nora
> Vargas was present to vote on behalf of the County. Chair Vargas lives in
> Chula Vista according to her profile.
>
> *Questions from APG members*
> *No questions received this month.*
>
> Original SANDAG documents and meeting minutes are available directly from
> SANDAG's Board of Directors website:
> https://www.sandag.org/meetings-and-events/board-of-directors
>
> Report Submitted by:
> Robin Joy Maxson, Representative to SANDAG
> Association of Planning Groups - San Diego County
>
>
```

```text
Good Evening APG Members,

Saturday, January 4th 2025, from 10:00am to noon, is the first APG meeting of the new year and this is our in-person meeting which means we need at least 10 voting members in attendance. If you are not able to attend, please send another member of your CPG to attend in-person.

Please respond to this email with your confirmation of attendance for the meeting.

Thank you for serving our unincorporated communities. And, see everyone next year!

Robin Joy Maxson, Chair

Association of Planning Groups - San Diego County
```

```text
Hi Travis-
I received the email below from the Clerk of the Board regarding Roger Garay's Form 700. He is saying he has not been a member of the Alpine CPG since 2021, however the attached Board Letter shows that he was re-nominated to his seat on the Alpine CPG just recently on December 10, 2024. Did you send his nomination to the Supervisor's office? I am not sure how he ended up being nominated again if he is saying he resigned years ago. Also, if he did resign at some point did he send in a letter of resignation? The Clerk of the Board needs this information as he can face legal consequences for not having filed his Form 700.

Also, do you have the meeting minutes for your CPG for June, September, October, and December 2024 to post on the PDS website? Thanks!

[cid:image002.png@01DB62A0.35C6C4F0]

Dawn Noble | Program Coordinator | Support Services
Planning & Development Services | Support Services
5510 Overland Avenue | Suite 210 | San Diego, CA 92123 MS: O-377

Sent: Thursday, January 9, 2025 1:06 PM
To: Noble, Dawn <Dawn.Noble@sdcounty.ca.gov>
Subject: Form700 Filer Roger Garay / Alpine Community Planning Group

Hello Dawn,

I not sure if I had sent question regarding filer Roger Garay before, I apologize if it is repetitive.  Our office received an email from FPPC with the following statement "I issued a letter to Roger Garay regarding their missing 2023 Annual. Garay called me today stating that they are no longer member, and that they haven't been on the Alpine Community Planning Group for a while - likely since they contracted COVID in 2021."  Please advise if the filer is no longer active filer and provide the leaving office date.  Please advise.  Thank you
```

```text
VAC25-001.

In 1989 a number of meetings were held between the owners  of APN
523-210-12 and other parcels of land that made up TM 4517. it was agreed
upon to preserve in permanent open space as indicated on TM4517, these
discussions included the County of San Diego

TM4517.
```

```text
> Subject: On behalf of SANDAG CEO Mario Orso: Legislative Update
> To:
> Cc: Clerk of the Board <clerkoftheboard@sandag.org>
>
>
> Dear Members of the Board,
>
> As mentioned at the Board of Directors meeting last Friday, I wanted to
> provide an update on two pieces of legislation recently introduced in the
> state legislature that, if enacted, would modify the composition of the
> SANDAG Board of Directors:
>
> *AB 24 (DeMaio)*
> This bill, introduced by Assemblymember Carl DeMaio, proposes modifying
> the SANDAG Board of Directors by removing one of the two County Board of
> Supervisors seats and replacing it with a representative from the San Diego
> County Association of Planning Groups. It would also require the remaining
> County Board representative to be from an unincorporated area of the
> county. The bill has been double-referred to the Assembly Local Government
> Committee and the Assembly Transportation Committee. It currently does not
> have a scheduled hearing date.
>
> *SB 240 (Jones)*
> This bill, introduced by Senate Minority Leader Brian Jones, proposes to
> replace the secondary representative from the County Board of Supervisors
> with a resident from an unincorporated area of the County, as selected by a
> majority of the county’s community planning groups, to serve a 1-year term.
> The bill also eliminates the requirement that one Board of Supervisors
> member be from a substantially incorporated district and the other from an
> unincorporated district. This bill has not yet been referred to a policy
> committee in the Senate.
>
> We will continue to monitor these bills and provide you with updates
> accordingly.
>
> Thank you,
> Mario
>
```

```text
<http://email.granicusideas.com/c/eJw8yztygzAQBuDTSJ2ZRQ8kFSrS-BqeP7sbm4lBjAXOkNOnywE-qYmKt1rHFAulFCjaR00lcYQgI-c8aUAsY0AKlEsRFrVznbKUKYwM-CS3OHkHJiaiL1KKibyDCYRf9O3SN8X3sflhwfy0z_rY960b_2Hc1bhrF27Hup_D_YV15qPPougDt8W-6mfrN-W2LLrulx-VVbvgNIH-FWO4t7d9V_cXAAD__451QFM>
New eComment for Board of Supervisors
Debra ONeill submitted a new eComment.

Meeting: Board of Supervisors

Item: 4. NOTICED PUBLIC HEARING: HARMONY GROVE VILLAGE SOUTH GENERAL PLAN AMENDMENT, SPECIFIC PLAN, ZONE RECLASSIFICATION, TENTATIVE MAP, MAJOR USE PERMIT, SITE PLAN AND ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT REPORT AND ASSOCIATED DOCUMENTS

eComment: HGVS is proposed on a single dead-end road, Country Club Drive, and asks for a waiver of both State and County fire codes requiring two or more exits on a dead-end road over 800 feet long. The entire neighborhood has been deemed a Very High Fire Severity Zone by Cal-Fire Maps. Does it really seem safe to place 1000 residents in a Very High Fire Severity Zone with only one exit, especially when Fire Code says it isn’t? In a Santa Ana wind driven fire, people are going to end up trapped on the road, or they will die in their homes because they think “fire hardened” means “fire safe.” In the Coco’s fire I evacuated past 6' high flames. I was able to evacuate because only about 75 of us lived on Country Club, not the 1000 or so HGVS would bring. At a Community Meeting on 9/17/25, RSFFPD Chief McQuead stated that HGVS would not be approved by the Fire Department now because there was no secondary exit. Why is this development being held to 2018 standards and not current standards?

View and Analyze eComments <http://email.granicusideas.com/c/eJw8zDtywyAQANDTQGfN8pcKijS6hme1LDYTAxojOeOcPlVcvualGGAxkqMKboEQLDh5jxuCQlDZzbAxZK1myjzbzTlPOXmWJfo5Ld4qQjQhXZ03GgkIADIwuABGo7CAvzj2y9gZv8_dTBXLQz7i_Th2Yb6EXoVeR6J-tuM93Z7YCp2jJMYxUa9Cr5hqaUKvTL1WbseQz7j1cf335YdT45HwLSx8JsLp1l_yFfVfAAAA___DTkdU>

This email was sent from https://sdcounty.granicusideas.com <http://email.granicusideas.com/c/eJw8yztygzAQBuDTSJ2ZRQ8kFSrS-BqeP7sbm4lBjAXOkNOnywE-qYmKt1rHFAulFCjaR00lcYQgI-c8aUAsY0AKlEsRFrVznbKUKYwM-CS3OHkHJiaiL1KKibyDCYRf9O3SN8X3sflhwfy0z_rY960b_2Hc1bhrF27Hup_D_YV15qPPougDt8W-6mfrN-W2LLrulx-VVbvgNIH-FWO4t7d9V_cXAAD__451QFM> .
```

```text
View email in your browser.

Farewell!

Dear Readers,

My last newsletter issue features the AJPH. It comprises
reflections on my tenure and the guidance for improving quality in
scientific publications developed over 2 years by a network led by
AJPH.

It was my honor and joy to serve as the Editor-in-Chief of the
AJPH.

Sincerely,
Alfredo Morabia, MD, PhD

Featured Articles
AJPH and Scientific Publications
Ten Years at the Helm: Reflections From the AJPH
Editor-in-Chief

From:                                                       Alfredo Morabia <apha@apha.org>
Sent:                                                         Friday, June 20, 2025 10:45 AM
To:                                                            Leroy, Chiara
Subject:                                                   [External] AJPH Weekly Newsletter - AJPH and Scientiﬁc Publications

Reflections from the AJPH Editor-in-Chief on the
last ten years at the helm.
Ensuring Quality in Scientific Publication Through
Inclusivity and Transparency

The Guidance for Ensuring Quality in Scientific
Publication Through Inclusivity and Transparency
(EQSPI) outlines key practices that journals can
adopt or adapt based on their unique capacities to
ensure researchers from all backgrounds have
access to the publishing process and that
publishing findings are presented accurately,
transparently, and reliably.
Podcasts
What is Public Health: Being a Nutritionist
In this series, "What is Public Health?", Vickie Mays and Alfredo Morabia interview
Marion Nestle, Paulette Goddard Professor of Nutrition, Food Studies, and Public Health,
Emerita, at New York University. Her most recent book is a memoir, Slow Cooked: An
Unexpected Life in Food Politics (2022), discusses the role of a public health nutritionist,
and how this role maybe impacted by the current changes at the Human and Health
Service agency.

Podcast: Listen to the audio | Watch the video
New in First Look
African Immigrants and the End the HIV Epidemic in the United States: Actionable
Recommendations to Enhance Equity
Preventing Real-World Pediatric Vehicular Heatstroke Events With Technology
Self-Managed Medication Abortion: History, Evidence, Models of Care, and Policy
Considerations
Latest AJPH Issue
July 2025
The Environmental, Health, and Humanitarian
Crises in Gaza

The July issue of AJPH features a special section on
conflict and the environment presented through the
lens of the current crisis in Gaza. Select articles
cover the environmental destruction of Gaza, Israel
and Gaza’s past cooperation in public health, and
opportunities for action.

Other articles in this issue address quality scientific
publication, detection of measles in Texas
wastewater, firearm injury hospitalizations, HIV
criminalization in the Black LGBTQIA+ community,
and more.
Read Issue
The mission of AJPH is to advance public health research, policy, practice, and
education. Toward that goal, the journal also produces monthly podcasts available in
English and Chinese at ajph.org. The monthly podcasts are also on iTunes and Google
Play.

Be on the lookout for more timely research from AJPH, and consider subscribing or
becoming an APHA member for full access.

Let’s be strong,

Alfredo Morabia, MD, PhD
Editor-in-Chief, AJPH
Home | About APHA | Annual Meeting | Registration | Plan | Program | Partners | Join APHA
American Public Health Association
800 I St. NW, Washington DC 20001
www.apha.org
202-777-2742

Unsubscribe from all future APHA emails. Please Note: This will remove you from all APHA communications, including
important updates, membership information and newsletters.
```

## Synthetic emails flagged by the verifier (35)

### S1 · 24-409 · [External] RE: Standing Meeting Acton Items

`synth:24-409:00:01`

- **Kind / recipe:** close_call_positive / marginal_mention — Responsive only because of a brief, passing reference that meets a request criterion (e.g. one line in an otherwise unrelated email); the rest of the email is about something else.
- **Intended label:** responsive (1)
- **Why (generator):** Responsive under item (4): in a Jan-Feb 2023 thread between Geosyntec (Stephanie Zinn, Megan Otto) and County staffer Elise Ruiz, the Feb 6 message briefly mentions uploading "the RWER working files"; the rest is about Sharepoint access and onboarding a subconsultant.
- **Verifier:** label ok = True, recipe ok = False, style tell = mild
- **Verifier's problem:** The rewrite adds body references to Geosyntec ('Geosyntec Deliverables folder', 'which Geosyntec staff and subs need edit access') in a Geosyntec to Elise Ruiz thread. That makes it responsive under item 4 without the RWER line, so it is not a marginal-mention close call. The why_label leaves this out. Also, the thread is cut off after Elise's 1/27 RE: message with nothing quoted under it, while that message refers back to the removed 'details from our Wednesday meeting'. Hi Elise,

```text
Just checking in on our inquiry below regarding Northern Baja regions to focus on?

Also, please see attached for a revised Stakeholder Interview List that has three phases described below.  I’d like to provide a short summary of our thinking behind this.  The graywater focus interviews are more technical in nature, so we feel like we can get those going sooner than later.  For the Task 2A longer list of stakeholders (15 to be interviewed), we didn’t want to start with too many as there are some decisions that still need to be made regarding the 15 to be interviewed.  Therefore, we only proposed 4 stakeholders on this list where we feel they would provide good input and would take less strategy to establish the interviews.  Most agricultural stakeholders are split between Phase 2 and Phase 3, we think the Phase 2 list could likely be reviewed by your team fairly quickly and would take less messaging strategy (e.g. for the national agriculture stakeholders that were proposed by re-focus).  We anticipate that the local agricultural stakeholders will take more strategy and are therefore listed under Phase 3.

  1.  Phase 1 – these stakeholders would require less strategy on messaging during the interview where Geosyntec/subs could be ready for interview scheduling now.
  2.  Phase 2 – these stakeholders require some strategy, and a meeting between Geosyntec/County is necessary to finalize messaging.
  3.  Phase 3 – these stakeholders require a lot of coordination between Geosyntec/County and require strategic messaging during the interviews.

Let’s plan to discuss this at our standing meeting on Wednesday and any questions you may have.  If possible, I think a good goal would be to get some of these Phase 1 stakeholder interviews going in a week from now to stay on schedule.  I’ll also plan to update the Sharepoint site with this revised matrix.

Thank you,
Stephanie
```

### S2 · 24-409 · RE: Graywater TM - Draft Annotated Outline

`synth:24-409:00:05`

- **Kind / recipe:** close_call_positive / marginal_mention — Responsive only because of a brief, passing reference that meets a request criterion (e.g. one line in an otherwise unrelated email); the rest of the email is about something else.
- **Intended label:** responsive (1)
- **Why (generator):** Responsive under item (4) (and item 3): a Mar 2023 County/Geosyntec thread (Elise Ruiz, Eden Brukman, Crystal Benham, Murtaza Baxamusa, Stephanie Gaines) about a graywater memo outline, with one passing line from Elise that "the RWER outline is still a few weeks out."
- **Verifier:** label ok = True, recipe ok = False, style tell = mild
- **Verifier's problem:** Elise's forward is centrally about a Geosyntec deliverable ('Geosyntec has provided OSEJ and DPW with an outline', 'bi-weekly team meeting with Geosyntec') and she is a named item-4 correspondent. So it is squarely responsive under item 4 whether or not the added line 'RWER outline is still a few weeks out' is there. Not a marginal close call. Also, 'Task 3.B_Graywater TM' conflicts with the real production, where Task 3.B is the RWER Facilities Inventory and Screening task.

```text
From: Lawrance, Christopher <Christopher.Lawrance@sdcounty.ca.gov <mailto:Christopher.Lawrance@sdcounty.ca.gov> >
Sent: Monday, December 5, 2022 4:53 PM
To: Gaines, Stephanie <Stephanie.Gaines@sdcounty.ca.gov <mailto:Stephanie.Gaines@sdcounty.ca.gov> >
Cc: Mohrlock, Charles <Charles.Mohrlock@sdcounty.ca.gov <mailto:Charles.Mohrlock@sdcounty.ca.gov> >
Subject: RE: Onsite Treatment and Reuse of Non-potable Water

Hi Stephanie,

Below are my notes on the proposed regulations for Onsite Treatment and Reuse of Non-potable Water.

Regulatory Objective

To establish risk-based log reduction targets for the removal of pathogens such as enteric viruses, parasitic protozoa, and enteric bacteria for non-potable water sources (graywater, rainwater, stormwater, and blackwater), and non-potable end uses (toilet and urinal flushing, clothes washing, irrigation, and dust suppression).

Non-potable water sources

Non-potable end uses

*	Graywater
*	Rainwater
*	Stormwater
*	Blackwater

*	Toilet Flushing
*	Clothes Washing
*	Irrigation
*	Dust Suppression

Exclusions

Regulations do not apply to:

*	Untreated graywater systems that are used exclusively for subsurface irrigation
*	Untreated rainwater systems that are used exclusively for surface, subsurface, or drip irrigation
*	Municipal stormwater capture and use systems

What systems are subject to the new regulations?

An onsite treatment system is subject to the new regulations if:

*	It treats one or more of the following water sources, in whole or in part:

	*	Rainwater
	*	Stormwater
	*	Graywater, or
	*	Blackwater

*	The water sources are collected, treated, and used onsite
*	The treated water is used for non-potable purpose
*	It is connected to community sewer system as its only mean for disposal (blackwater system)

The regulations are intended for building-scale or district-scale (multiple buildings or campus) systems onsite reuse.

Risk-based Water Quality Standard

The goal is to achieve a (microbial) risk level of health protection from pathogens in onsite treated water of 1 in 10,000 infections per person per year. This is consistent with acceptable risk for EPA drinking water regulations and State of CA potable reuse regulations.

There will be two sets of log reduction targets for 1) indoor uses (toilet flushing, clothes washing) and 2) outdoor uses (irrigation, dust control, fire suppression, and car washing).

Schedule

The State Water Board is required to adopt the regulations by December 1, 2022. The Department of Housing and Community Development is required to develop and propose for adoption any necessary corresponding building standards to support the regulations adopted by the State Water Board by December 1, 2023.

The State Water Board’s website <https://www.waterboards.ca.gov/drinking_water/certlic/drinkingwater/onsite_nonpotable_reuse_regulations.html>  for the program offers no evidence that the regulation have been finalized. All procedural dates are listed as TBD.

Let me know if you have any questions you’d like me to look into.

Thanks,

Chris

From: Sandy Mathews <sandym@lwa.com <mailto:sandym@lwa.com> >
Sent: Tuesday, November 8, 2022 4:47 PM
To: Clapper, Kacen <kacen.clapper@wsp.com <mailto:kacen.clapper@wsp.com> >; Gaines, Stephanie <Stephanie.Gaines@sdcounty.ca.gov <mailto:Stephanie.Gaines@sdcounty.ca.gov> >
Cc: Ashli Desai <ashlid@lwa.com <mailto:ashlid@lwa.com> >
Subject: [External] Onsite Treatment and Reuse of Non-potable Water

Kacen, Stephanie,

Here is some follow-up information from the Policy and Permitting Subcommittee call. The Division of Drinking Water is developing regulations for the Onsite Treatment and Reuse of Non-potable Water. This is a legislative mandate. California Water Code section 13558 requires the State Water Board to adopt regulations on or before December 1, 2022. I just checked the website, and the schedule is still listed as TBD.

There was a workshop earlier this year and several stormwater representatives participated asking a lot of questions about whether this will affect our capture and use projects. The outcome was including the following statement in the FAQ, which is somewhat reassuring, however this might be something to track as the underlying language in the legislation and the draft information shared remains confusing.

12. Will the proposed regulations apply to municipal stormwater capture and use systems? No. The regulations are intended for building-scale or district-scale (multiple buildings or campus) systems onsite reuse. Watershed-scale stormwater capture projects designed to collect and store watershed-wide runoff during dry or wet weather conditions would not fit into the definition of OTNWS since the collection of the source water (stormwater) and in many cases, the use, are not onsite.

See more info here:  https://www.waterboards.ca.gov/drinking_water/certlic/drinkingwater/onsite_nonpotable_reuse_regulations.html <https://www.waterboards.ca.gov/drinking_water/certlic/drinkingwater/onsite_nonpotable_reuse_regulations.html>

Sandy
```

```text
Subject: [External] RWER Task 3.B - Facilities Inventory and Screening Submittal

Hi Stephanie,

The Task 3.B deliverable is on Sharepoint in the Task 3.B folder <https://projectsitesb.geosyntec.com/8/SW033129-0547/_layouts/15/start.aspx#/SitePages/Home.aspx?RootFolder=%2F8%2FSW033129-0547%2FShared%20Documents%2FTask%203%20-%20SW%20Capture%20and%20Use%20at%20County%20Parks%20and%20Facilities%2FTask%203B%20Deliverable&FolderCTID=0x012000BB8DC2F928EF0944BBEB156EFA200989&View=%7B09ED6256-943F-4252-81B3-27AB4979A7B9%7D> . As discussed on the call today, the table presents the attributes associated with each County facility, supporting the potential infiltration and diversion screening as well as the next Task 3.C's prioritization and identification of potential project opportunities. These facilities are compiled from the Task 3.A deliverable and include the additional facilities you sent on 7/3 from the missing data we identified during that task. The following two tabs are included for the Task 3.B deliverable:

*	Attributes, Rationale, and Desc: This tab provides the rationale, sources, and description of the various attributes added to each facility.
*	FAC_INF_DIV_CAP_RESULTS: This tab provides the attribute results for each facility, along with the infiltration and diversion screening results. The initial screening criteria for feasible infiltration and diversion for water supply in Columns O and P are listed below. Onsite Capture and Use screening will be shown during Task 3.C. Additionally, we have included if the parcel overlaps with a SWCFS Parcel, has dry weather flows at MS4 outfalls within 500ft sourced from WSP, and has water demands based on the data provided by the County in May.

Task 3.C will identify the potential project opportunities based on additional applied criteria to rule out areas not ideal for infiltration, diversion, or onsite capture and use (e.g., not within 40ft of septic parcels or 100ft of contamination sites, already has recycled water etc.). and include the weights and resulting scores for each of the facilities along with the prioritized list based on the prioritization framework.

It would be helpful to know if you would like us to remove leased facilities or if there are any other facilities we should remove prior to the prioritization and identification of potential project opportunities. Additionally, let us know if you would like us to include any other attributes for these facilities to support Task 3.C. Please provide this information by July 21 if possible.

1.	Infiltration

	a.	Infiltration screening

		*	Groundwater basins within 1-mile buffer, or;
		*	Within 1-Mile of a drinking well, but outside 150 feet of a drinking well, and;
		*	Permeable geology (Alluvium, Basalt, Conglomerate, Sandstone), and;
		*	Slopes <15%.

2.	Diversion

	a.	Diversion screening

		*	Within sewershed to recycled water treatment plant

Please let us know if you have questions!

Thank you!
```

```text
Subject: [External] RE: Standing Meeting Acton Items

Hi Elise,

Just checking in on our inquiry below regarding Northern Baja regions to focus on?

Also, please see attached for a revised Stakeholder Interview List that has three phases described below.  I’d like to provide a short summary of our thinking behind this.  The graywater focus interviews are more technical in nature, so we feel like we can get those going sooner than later.  For the Task 2A longer list of stakeholders (15 to be interviewed), we didn’t want to start with too many as there are some decisions that still need to be made regarding the 15 to be interviewed.  Therefore, we only proposed 4 stakeholders on this list where we feel they would provide good input and would take less strategy to establish the interviews.  Most agricultural stakeholders are split between Phase 2 and Phase 3, we think the Phase 2 list could likely be reviewed by your team fairly quickly and would take less messaging strategy (e.g. for the national agriculture stakeholders that were proposed by re-focus).  We anticipate that the local agricultural stakeholders will take more strategy and are therefore listed under Phase 3.

  1.  Phase 1 – these stakeholders would require less strategy on messaging during the interview where Geosyntec/subs could be ready for interview scheduling now.
  2.  Phase 2 – these stakeholders require some strategy, and a meeting between Geosyntec/County is necessary to finalize messaging.
  3.  Phase 3 – these stakeholders require a lot of coordination between Geosyntec/County and require strategic messaging during the interviews.

Let’s plan to discuss this at our standing meeting on Wednesday and any questions you may have.  If possible, I think a good goal would be to get some of these Phase 1 stakeholder interviews going in a week from now to stay on schedule.  I’ll also plan to update the Sharepoint site with this revised matrix.

Thank you,
Stephanie

Hi Elise,

Thanks for the great call today.  I’ll be uploading the meeting notes to the Sharepoint site so all can see main discussion points.  Also, we were able to locate that Board Letter so I will be reviewing it this week to help better understand the history.  In the meantime, please see below for action items:

County

  *   Communication protocol for DEHQ & PDS + departmental contacts (for graywater / stormwater capture interviews) I’ve been in touch with department directors and waiting for responses - will have more for you early next week.
  *   Provide thoughts on Military / Tribes /Water Agency stakeholders to interview + preferred cities/entities for bi-national interviews (Mexicali and Tijuana over Valle de Guadalupe) I’ve met with our internal outreach team and got more direction with tribal and binational contacts. I will brief you on these updates during our next meeting. I have one contact for military and am in the process of doing more research.
  *   Confirmation that it is acceptable to interview stakeholders outside of California (e.g. Arizona graywater practices) I spoke with Murtaza and he advises that as long as this is for research/exploratory purposes based on the subject matter it is ok to contact outside of the San Diego region.

Geosyntec

  *   Provide draft interview list early next week (~1/23)
  *   Send invite for 30 minute standing meeting (best option appears to be Wednesday 1-130)
  *   Send the following:

     *   UCSD referenced studies + their dates
     *   Chula Vista Healthier Cities link
     *   List of potential elected officials
     *   List of Labor/Workforce recommended stakeholders specifically for Murtaza review

Please let me know if I missed anything.

Thank you,
Stephanie
```

```text
Dear Travis Lyon,

My name is Michelle Torby, and I am an intern with the Institute for Public
Strategies (IPS). IPS is a non-profit organization that focuses on the
prevention of binge and underage drinking in San Diego County.

We are collecting responses to a short community survey about alcohol
availability near schools and parks in East County. The survey is anonymous
and takes about 10 minutes. We are asking local residents to fill it out and
share it with neighbors who may be interested, and I would be happy to send
you the link.

If it is alright with you, I could also bring a few flyers with the survey
to the next Alpine Community Planning Group meeting.

We would appreciate any help getting the word out. Thank you for your
consideration, and I look forward to hearing from you.

Best regards,

Michelle Torby
(248) 762-9312
```

```text
Dear Travis Lyon,

My name is Michelle Torby, and I am an intern with the Institute for Public
Strategies (IPS). IPS is a non-profit organization that focuses on the
prevention of binge and underage drinking in San Diego County.

Our organization would like to present at the Alpine Community Planning
Group monthly meeting. We would like to do a 15-20 minute capacity building
workshop for members of the Planning Group regarding the role that planning
groups have in preventing excessive alcohol outlet density which
contributes to binge and underage drinking.

We would appreciate any information on how to participate in this meeting.
Thank you for your consideration, and I look forward to hearing from you.

Best regards,

Michelle Torby
(248) 762-9312
```

```text
Good morning,

Thank you for your call last week. I looked up the parcel next to your home on our eligibility map. It is shown in blue, which means three or more units can be built there under current Zoning, and a multifamily project on that parcel could be eligible for streamlining under State law if it met all of the eligibility requirements. Being shown on the map does not mean a project has been proposed, and no applications for this streamlined process have been submitted in your Community Planning Area.  If you would like more detail on the zoning or permit history for that property, the PDS Zoning Counter would be the best place to start.

Also, a reminder that we are still requesting your group's feedback on the Alpine checklist by August 15th, 2024.

Please let me know if you have any other questions.
```

<details><summary>Original email it was edited from</summary>

```text
Good morning,

Thank you for your continued coordination with our Objective Design Standards (ODS) project. As discussed, State laws that are already in effect require that any housing developments eligible for streamlining approval be reviewed with clear, objective standards regarding design aspects such as materials, colors, etc. Typically, these would be projects that include affordable housing units.  Design standards that are not objective cannot be applied to these projects.

To ensure we have objective design standards available for any potential applications, our team used your existing Design Review Checklist as a template to create an Objective Design Review Checklist that clarifies any subjective language and maintains the visual integrity of housing developments in your community. We kindly request your group send any feedback on the checklist by August 15th, 2024.

The Objective Design Review Checklist will serve as a supplemental form for projects eligible for streamlined approval under state law. It will not replace the current Design Review Checklist<https://www.sandiegocounty.gov/content/dam/sdc/pds/drb/AlpineDesignReviewChecklistFINAL1.pdf>, which will continue to apply to projects not eligible for the streamlined review. While no applications for this streamlined process have been submitted in your Community Planning Area, the Objective Design Review Checklist will be applicable if one is received. We are finalizing this Objective Design Review Checklist and will make it easily accessible online for potential applications eligible for these State laws.

Attached is the draft Objective Design Review version of your community’s existing Design Review Checklist, and a map delineating potentially eligible areas for this streamlined process. A strikethrough indicates a proposed removal of language, while an underline indicates a proposed change/addition.  Also attached is a PDF version for your reference. There may also be highlighted language or comment bubbles that we are requesting specific feedback on, regarding unclear language or unclear measurements.  There are additional details in the original e-mail below.

We will work to incorporate your suggestions into the checklist which will be accessible on our website in Fall 2024. While there is some flexibility to make changes in the future, staff availability and allocation may make it challenging to respond urgently to suggestions for edits after that date. Therefore, please ensure you submit any requests for modifications by August 15th, 2024, so your feedback can be incorporated into the checklist before it is made available online.

Please let us know if you have any questions as you review the document. We appreciate your continued collaboration and commitment to enhancing our unincorporated communities.

[cid:image001.png@01DA7ABA.EF015A80]<http://www.sdcpds.org/>
Luis Duran
Planner III | Planning & Development Services | Long Range Planning
County of San Diego | 5510 Overland Ave. Suite 310 | San Diego, CA 92123|
• (619) 214-4698  |  • Luis.Duran@sdcounty.ca.gov<mailto:Luis.Duran@sdcounty.ca.gov>

From: Duran, Luis
Sent: Friday, March 22, 2024 4:03 PM
To: travislyonacpg@gmail.com
Subject: Objective Design Standards (ODS) Checklist - Alpine

Hello Chair Lyon,

I hope this email finds you well. Following our recent presentation at your Community Planning Group (CPG) meeting on February 22, 2024. I am pleased to update you on the latest progress regarding the Objective Design Standards (ODS) project.

To recap, our presentation centered around State laws which allow for a streamlined process for eligible multifamily projects, and how design standards can be implemented in that process. Eligible projects need to meet all eligibility requirements and objective design standards at the time of application, or their development cannot be streamlined through a ministerial process. Our project team is working to edit your existing Design Review Checklist to clarify any subjective language to maintain the visual integrity of your community in the case an eligible project application is submitted. Attached is a draft “Objective” version of the Alpine Checklist with a corresponding map, delineating potentially eligible areas for streamlined housing development.

A few notes as you review the attached documents:

MAP: State law states that a multifamily development “contains two or more residential units”.  According to the California Department of Housing and Community Development, a new single-family development and an attached accessory dwelling unit when built together are considered multifamily and are eligible for streamlining under SB 35.  An applicant would still need to meet all eligibility requirements to streamline their project. These eligible parcels are distinguished by a pink color.  The blue parcels are eligible for streamlining under State law, where three or more units can be built under current Zoning. The attached maps have been updated to reflect recent State laws in effect as of March 2024.

CHECKLIST: Attached is a Microsoft Word document containing tracked changes.  A strikethrough indicates a proposed removal of language, while an underline indicates proposed change/addition.  Also attached is a PDF version for your reference. The Design Review Checklist for your community may include goals or intentions related to your original Design Guidelines. As this draft is for ease of use, we have removed these to strictly focus on the design standards that must be followed. This makes the checklist shorter and easier to read. There may also be highlighted language or comment bubbles that we are requesting specific feedback on, regarding unclear language or unclear measurements.

As discussed previously, this project will not be updating or changing Alpine’s Form Based Code (FBC).  The Draft Objective Design Standards Checklist attached is for the areas not covered by the FBC.  We kindly request your group review the attached Checklist to ensure that it accurately reflects the intent of your existing community’s design standards. Please submit your initial feedback no later than April 23rd, 2024.

We appreciate your collaboration and commitment to enhancing our unincorporated communities.

For further information on ODS, please visit the following links: Objective Design Standards | Engage San Diego County<https://engage.sandiegocounty.gov/ods> and Objective Design Standards (sandiegocounty.gov)<https://www.sandiegocounty.gov/content/sdc/pds/longrangeplanning/ods/>

[cid:image001.png@01DA7ABA.EF015A80]<http://www.sdcpds.org/>
Luis Duran
Planner III | Planning & Development Services | Long Range Planning
County of San Diego | 5510 Overland Ave. Suite 310 | San Diego, CA 92123|
• (619) 214-4698  |  • Luis.Duran@sdcounty.ca.gov<mailto:Luis.Duran@sdcounty.ca.gov>
```

</details>

```text
Hi Travis -
Please see below updates made to the Alpine CPG membership:

Reappointments and New Member
Please see attached the Minute Order from the 12/10/24 Board of Supervisors Meeting with the reappointments for the members whose seats are expiring on 1/6/2025, highlighted on page 2. The Clerk of the Board mailed letters to you and Nina Gould (new member) confirming your appointments and seat numbers since you both signed up for the election as candidates. Nina was also sent an email from PDS with the training requirements for new members to complete.

Expiring Seats
Mary Brochard- seat 1 and Michael Milligan-seat 11 did not apply to renew their membership and their terms will expire on 1/6/25. The Clerk of the Board will be sending them their leaving office Form 700, which they will have 30 days from the date their seats expire on 1/6/25 to complete.  Please let me know if there are any changes to the status of these two members.

Updated Roster
The roster for the Alpine CPG has been updated and is posted on the PDS website here: https://www.sandiegocounty.gov/content/dam/sdc/pds/Groups/alpine/ALROSTER.pdf
Please review and let me know if any changes are needed.

Vacancies and pending applicants
There are now two vacant seats on the Alpine CPG - Seat 2 and 11. - **Attachments:** image001.png, image004.png, image002.png, image003.png
```

```text
Nicholas Brust

Deputy District Director

CA Senator Brian Jones

Good Morning, Mr. Brust,

Thank you for working on this meeting for the trails and other Loveland community priorities, such as fish restocking, reasonable public access hours that server working people and families, and a balance of recreation and wildlife protection in conjunction with water storage policies.

I would like to add that some things are not allowed : 

1}Contractors interested in bidding projects are excluded from steering the proposals.

2)Proposals that push AB 1O2 funds through any brand, for instance, a nonprofit or commercial interest.

One exception might be costs associated with paperwork or title work/  an escrow to incorporate the SDGE easements into public access trails. 

What I would like to suggest:

1) Before the meeting: Clarity from Congressman Issa's office on the legality and restoration of  Forest Service easement-protected hours of Sunrise to Sunset and the encumbrance of the fishing program on Sweetwater's minimum pool. 

2) Invite an SDGE public affairs specialist to discuss the public use of the  Sunrise Powerlink easement roads at Loveland.

3) A neutral subject matter expert contractor on trails, fishing infrastructure,  and other related matters who is in an advisory role only and will not unduly steer or bid on proposals.

4) Participation from a  Regional Waterboards  permitting expert to help define or give feedback on impacts that water regulation standards will or won’t have on our proposals. Confusion on these matters has been stifling in previous meetings.

5) A rubric for choosing proposals created by the Loveland stakeholders in attendance or designed by Mr. Jantz with consideration for feedback from Sweetwater Authority and Loveland advocates/stakeholders.

6) A report back on this meeting at the Alpine Community Planning Group's March 28 meeting.

Please let me know if I can assist you or others in Senator Jones's office with this important meeting. 

Appreciatively,

Russell Walsh

4639 Montiel Truck Trail

Jamul CA, 91935

619-922-6235

    On Monday, February 26, 2024 at 02:55:32 PM PST, Brust, Nicholas <nicholas.brust@sen.ca.gov> wrote:

  <!--#yiv7210241357 filtered {}#yiv7210241357 filtered {}#yiv7210241357 filtered {}#yiv7210241357 p.yiv7210241357MsoNormal, #yiv7210241357 li.yiv7210241357MsoNormal, #yiv7210241357 div.yiv7210241357MsoNormal {margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;font-size:11.0pt;font-family:"Calibri", sans-serif;}#yiv7210241357 a:link, #yiv7210241357 span.yiv7210241357MsoHyperlink {color:#0563C1;text-decoration:underline;}#yiv7210241357 a:visited, #yiv7210241357 span.yiv7210241357MsoHyperlinkFollowed {color:#954F72;text-decoration:underline;}#yiv7210241357 span.yiv7210241357EmailStyle17 {font-family:"Calibri", sans-serif;color:windowtext;}#yiv7210241357 .yiv7210241357MsoChpDefault {font-family:"Calibri", sans-serif;}#yiv7210241357 filtered {}#yiv7210241357 div.yiv7210241357WordSection1 {}-->

We’re all set for the Loveland Trail Improvements public meeting at the Alpine Woman’s Club on March 14th from 5:00PM – 7:00PM. Barry Jantz has agreed to facilitate this meeting – we are in discussions now as to the format of the meeting and how to best serve both parties and welcome any suggestions. The Office of Senator Brian W. Jones is sending out a newsletter blast with the flyer provided by Sweetwater Authority. I will be sending out additional updates early next week. If I’ve left anyone off this email, please feel free to forward.

  

Sincerely,

  

Nick Brust

Deputy District Director

Senate Minority Leader Brian W. Jones

California State Senate 40th District

10650 Treena Street #110| San Diego, CA 92131
Office (858) 547-3818 | Mobile (760) 277-4220

  

Follow Senator Brian W. Jones on:

   

https://jones.cssrc.us/
```

### S25 · 25-3152 · Feasibility Study Interview Request - California State Water Resources Control Board 

`synth:25-3152:13:09`

- **Kind / recipe:** close_call_positive / marginal_mention — Responsive only because of a brief, passing reference that meets a request criterion (e.g. one line in an otherwise unrelated email); the rest of the email is about something else.
- **Intended label:** responsive (1)
- **Why (generator):** Item 4: Marissa Twite of Katz & Associates, a consultant to the State Water Board, writes to ACPG Chair Travis Lyon on 3/14/2025, inside the window, asking to interview him as an Alpine resident, and its only ACPG business is one line requesting time on an upcoming ACPG agenda.
- **Verifier:** label ok = True, recipe ok = False, style tell = none
- **Verifier's problem:** A consultant to the State Water Board writes directly to travislyonacpg@gmail.com on 3/14/2025, which is item 3 or 4 on the headers alone. The added 'Separately, ... time on an upcoming ACPG agenda' line is not the hook, so this is not a close call. The edit ('As an Alpine resident' plus one added sentence) is natural and keeps the original's dangling-modifier syntax.
- **From:** Marissa Twite <mtwite@katzandassociates.com>
- **To:** "travislyonacpg@gmail.com" <travislyonacpg@gmail.com>
- **Date:** 2025-03-14T23:43:04+00:00 (window: in)
- **Attachments:** image001.png, image002.png, image003.png, image004.png

```text
Good afternoon,

My name is Marissa, with Katz & Associates, and I'm part of a team under the California State Water Resources Control Board that is studying the feasibility of consolidating properties with individual groundwater wells or independent water systems east of Alpine with the Padre Dam Municipal Water District.

I'm reaching out today to see if you're interested in participating in an interview that will inform a Feasibility Study.

Background
The California State Water Resources Control Board<https://www.waterboards.ca.gov/> (Board) under the Safe and Affordable Funding for Equity and Resilience (Safer) Drinking Water Program<https://www.waterboards.ca.gov/water_issues/programs/grants_loans/sustainable_water_solutions/safer.html>, is funding planning efforts, including a Feasibility Study (Study), to support communities and help ensure a safe and reliable water supply for the future.

Due to some water quality concerns of existing groundwater wells in the Willowside Terrace community, located just east of the city of Alpine, the Board is conducting a Study to determine if this community can be connected to the Padre Dam Municipal Water District<https://www.padredam.org/> (PDMWD) to ensure a high quality, reliable and sustainable water supply is available for the future.

The Study team is interested in forming an expanded study area to include additional properties near Willowside Terrace that also use an independent water system reliant on groundwater.

As an Alpine resident, the Study team is interested in participating in an interview with you to gather insights into the communities east of Alpine, near the potential study area. The Study team is interested in a range of views, opinions and experiences about independent water systems, the services provided by PDMWD, and the possibility of consolidating independent water systems with the district

Please let me know if you are interested in participating in an interview and what your availability looks like for the next few weeks. Separately, we would like to request time on an upcoming Alpine Community Planning Group agenda for a brief update on the Study.

I look forward to hearing from you.

Marissa Twite (she/her/hers)
Account Supervisor
Phone 858.833.5392
[cid:image001.png@01DB9500.276E99E0]<https://www.swspr.com/>
[cid:image002.png@01DB9500.276E99E0]<https://www.facebook.com/southweststrategies/>
[cid:image003.png@01DB9500.276E99E0]<https://www.linkedin.com/company/southwest-strategies/>
[cid:image004.png@01DB9500.276E99E0]<https://twitter.com/SWSPR>
swspr.com

This email may contain information that is privileged, confidential or exempt  from disclosure under applicable law and is for the sole use of the intended recipient. Any review, reliance, copying, distribution or use of the contents is prohibited. If you are not the intended recipient, please contact the sender and delete all copies. Thank you.
```

### S28 · 25-3152 · RE: Rooftop Lease Amendment - CAL02204

`synth:25-3152:15:02`

- **Kind / recipe:** hard_negative / same_people_different_subject — Keep the same sender/recipients (people the request names or roster members); change the subject matter entirely to something personal or administrative that the request does not cover.
- **Intended label:** not responsive (0)
- **Why (generator):** Although these md7 emails to Chair Lyon fall inside the window, they are misaddressed correspondence about a rooftop cell-site lease on a building Lyon says he does not own or manage, with nothing about ACPG business, so they are not an item 3 or 4 communication about the group's business.
- **Verifier:** label ok = False, recipe ok = True, style tell = mild
- **Verifier's problem:** Responsive under item 4. md7 is an entity writing to Chair Lyon from April to October 2024. md7 and site CAL02204 are the applicant and site of the real PDS2024-MUP-24-003, so md7 is exactly the kind of 'entity with business before the group' that item 4 names. Changing the topic to a lease does not help. Internal inconsistency: in April Lyon says he is the wrong Travis and Harold promises to update the contact records. Then on 9/26 Harold writes to him again as the building contact about a countersigned amendment and crew access to the roof hatch.

```text
From: Nehl, Brandon <Brandon.Nehl@sdcounty.ca.gov<mailto:Brandon.Nehl@sdcounty.ca.gov>>
Date: Mon, Feb 26, 2024 at 1:17 PM
Subject: PDS2024-MUP-24-003 Major Use Permit
To: travislyonacpg@gmail.com<mailto:travislyonacpg@gmail.com> <travislyonacpg@gmail.com<mailto:travislyonacpg@gmail.com>>, cdean@benderdean.com<mailto:cdean@benderdean.com> <cdean@benderdean.com<mailto:cdean@benderdean.com>>
Cc: Aponte, Enrique <Enrique.Aponte@sdcounty.ca.gov<mailto:Enrique.Aponte@sdcounty.ca.gov>>

Attached are documents pertaining to a Discretionary Permit for PDS2024-MUP-24-003 Major Use Permit.  If you normally receive a plot plan, it will be delivered through regular mail within the next few days.
Pursuant to Board Policy I-1,
(https://www.sandiegocounty.gov/content/dam/sdc/cob/docs/policy/I-1.pdf), this request for a recommendation is being sent to you because a discretionary permit application has been submitted to Planning & Development Services (PDS) for a project within your Community/Design Review Area.  Once a recommendation is made, please forward it to PDS with Form #534 so the recommendation can be included in correspondence to the project applicant and as part of the staff report for the appropriate hearing body of officer.

Every effort should be made to submit a group recommendation within seven calendar days following the CPG/DRB/CSG meeting.
```

```text
Dear Travis Lyon,

My name is Michelle Torby, and I am a college student home for the summer.
One of your neighbors mentioned that you are looking for someone to watch
your dogs while you are away over the Fourth of July weekend.

families in the area and can come by twice a day to feed and walk them,
or stay at the house overnight if that works better for you.

I would appreciate any information on the dates and what you would need.
Thank you for your consideration, and I look forward to hearing from you.

Best regards,

Michelle Torby
```

```text
Dear Travis Lyon,

My name is Michelle Torby, and I am an intern with the Institute for Public
Strategies (IPS). IPS is a non-profit organization that focuses on the
prevention of binge and underage drinking in San Diego County.

Our organization would like to present at the Alpine Community Planning
Group monthly meeting. We would like to do a 15-20 minute capacity building
workshop for members of the Planning Group regarding the role that planning
groups have in preventing excessive alcohol outlet density which
contributes to binge and underage drinking.

We would appreciate any information on how to participate in this meeting.
Thank you for your consideration, and I look forward to hearing from you.

Best regards,

Michelle Torby
(248) 762-9312
```
